──────────────────────────────────────

I made this short film with Higgsfield, and here’s the full workflow: character references, scene prompts, and the takes used in the final edit. I’ve also included the bridge, extra club shots, and finale that weren’t covered in the video breakdown.

Watch the YouTube video first, then follow the workflow below.

Before You Start

Build your characters, locations, and props as Elements. Use video elements for Adam, Ron, and Victor; keep the same names in Claude and Higgsfield. Victor’s wound is a separate image reference.

The Skills Behind the Workflow

Tell Claude what happens; the skills turn your direction into video prompts. Use the scene skill for shots, the fight skill for choreography, and the acting skill for performance.

Element Names

Attach each asset to its matching name before generating. Use these exact names in Claude and Higgsfield.

@char_adam_video_v1 — Adam — video element
@char_ron_video_v1 — Ron — video element
@char_victor_video_v1 — Victor — video element
@char_victor_werewolf_image_v1 — Transformed Victor — image element
@char_victor_wound_image_v1 — Wound placement and size — image element
@loc_alley_v1, @loc_room_ron_v1, @loc_bridge_v1, @loc_club_exterior_v1, @loc_club_dancefloor_v1, @loc_corridor_v1 — Location images
@loc_room_victor_sofa_v1, @loc_room_victor_door_v1 — Two sides of Victor’s office
@char_club_guard_1_image_v1, @char_club_guard_2_image_v1, @char_club_guard_3_image_v1, @char_corridor_guards_image_v1, @char_dj_image_v1 — Supporting cast references
@prop_bike, @prop_adam_pistol, @prop_electric_baton, @prop_spent_bullet — Prop references
@image_1 … @image_5, @video_1 — Direct inputs; attach in the order specified by each prompt
Stage 1 — Build the Assets
Adam — From a Photo to a Character

Adam started with my photo: white hair, pale skin, an athletic build, a long black leather coat, and silver accessories. I tested curly and straight hair while keeping the costume and sheet layout consistent.

What it does: creates two alternative design sheets from the same photo. These are design inputs; the reusable scene reference is the video element made from the approved character.

Curly-Hair Design · GPT Image 2.5

What it does: Creates Adam’s curly-hair character sheet while keeping the face, costume, and two-panel layout consistent.

Adam — curly hair

Use the attached image as the strict identity reference for the man's facial structure: bone structure, face shape, eyes, nose, lips, mustache shape, curl pattern, haircut and facial proportions must stay clearly recognizable as the same person. Push the face harder and more rugged as described below while keeping those features. His coloring, hair color, height and body differ from the reference — build them as described; the reference man's dark hair and skin tone do not appear in the result.




Two studio photographs of the same young man with albinism arranged side by side as one film character sheet on a single continuous flat seamless cool light-grey studio backdrop, a thin clean gap between the two panels. On the left a full-body photograph, on the right a close-up head-and-shoulders portrait, the same real man in both panels, identical face, hair, skin, jewelry and wardrobe, consistent across panels. Both panels lit by the same hard directional key light from front-left with crisp shadow edges and deep falloff on the far side of the face, clean neutral cinematic look with the black leather held rich and unclipped.




The man is in his mid twenties, about 180 cm tall, dense athletic build with squared heavy shoulders, thick neck, broad chest, flat stomach, strong forearms with visible veins on the hands, long legs. He has albinism: very pale milky skin with a soft pink undertone and a faint pink flush on the cheeks, nose and ears, fine natural skin texture with visible pores and slightly rougher skin on the cheeks, a few faint pale freckles across the nose. Dense short tight corkscrew curls in pure white with a faint warm ivory tint, every strand fully white from root to tip, the same curl pattern and haircut as the reference, cropped tighter at the sides and fuller on top, a few loose coils falling forward over the split eyebrow. White eyebrows, the left eyebrow split by a thin pale healed scar, white eyelashes, a thin neat white mustache and a small tuft of white hair under the lower lip, joined by a light dusting of short white stubble along the jaw and chin, every hair on the face white. Very pale ice-blue eyes with a violet-grey cast set a little deeper under a heavier brow, a hard narrowed gaze straight into the lens, head tilted slightly down so he looks out from under the brow. Central Asian facial structure: the same oval face as the reference but with harder cheekbones, slightly hollowed cheeks, a small bump on the bridge of the nose as if once broken, full lower lip with a tiny pale healed nick, a sharper set jawline, small dark beauty marks on the cheeks in the same places as the reference. A small silver hoop earring in each ear plus a plain silver cuff on the upper cartilage of the left ear. Jaw set, mouth closed, an unbothered intimidating calm. No darker roots, no grey or blonde tones anywhere in the hair.




Around his neck a heavy polished silver curb chain with thick chunky links about a finger's width, worn over the t-shirt and lying on the upper chest in the open V of the coat. A pair of small round sunglasses with thin silver frames and dark lenses hooked by one arm into the collar of the t-shirt. Silver rings matching the chain: on his left hand a chunky oval silver signet ring with a smooth softly worn flat face on the ring finger, a wide plain flat silver band on the index finger and a thin silver band on the little finger; on his right thumb a heavy silver ring with a subtly hammered surface. All the silver polished with a soft patina in the recesses. Clean natural hands with five fingers each.




Wardrobe, identical in both panels: a floor-length black leather trench coat reaching the ankles, double-breasted with wide sharp peak lapels, black leather-covered buttons, structured broad shoulders, a straight heavy fall with soft vertical creasing, smooth matte-sheen leather with a subtle grain, worn fully open. Under it a plain faded washed-black crew-neck t-shirt with a slightly frayed collar, tucked in. Wide-leg black leather trousers in glossy smooth lambskin, high-waisted, relaxed straight cut with heavy fluid drape and deep vertical folds, puddling slightly over the boots. A slim white leather belt with a large ornate polished silver western buckle, a polished silver wallet chain clipped to the belt and looping down into the right front pocket. Black pointed-toe leather western boots with tonal stitching and a low angled heel, mostly hidden by the trouser hem. On his right wrist a black leather cuff with a single silver stud over a thin silver chain bracelet, a black bandana knotted around his left wrist.




The left panel is the full-body photograph: 50mm lens at chest height, full figure from head to boots with a little room above the head and the coat hem fully in frame, a soft shadow pooling under his boots, anatomically correct proportions with the head roughly one-eighth of his total height and shoulders clearly broader than the head, standing upright and planted, weight even on both feet, his right hand hooked into the front trouser pocket with the ringed thumb out, his left hand resting on the belt buckle with the fingers spread and the rings visible.




The right panel is the close-up portrait: 85mm lens at eye level, framed from just above the top of the curls down to the upper chest, his face filling most of the panel, shallow depth of field with the eyes tack sharp and a sharp catchlight in each, the chain and the sunglasses in the collar visible, the peak lapels and structured shoulders of the coat framing the edges of the panel, the split eyebrow scar, the stubble, the beauty marks and the ear cuff clearly readable.




Palette of 70% black leather and washed-black cotton, 18% cool neutral grey backdrop, 12% pale white skin, white hair, the white belt and polished silver as the brightest values in the frame. Cinematic film still texture with fine organic grain, true-to-life proportions, no visible logos, patches or text, no captions or labels on the sheet.

Add Real Texture

What it does: Provides separate references for the hair, skin, background, and T-shirt texture.

Once the design works, improve the surfaces. I use separate references for the hair, skin, background, and T-shirt. Each reference has one job, so the model gets a concrete visual target.

Attach in order: @image_1 approved Adam sheet; @image_2 hair texture; @image_3 skin texture; @image_4 background; @image_5 black T-shirt fabric.

Hair texture

Texture Refinement · Seedream 5.0 Pro

What it does: Refines Adam’s approved character sheet using the four texture references.

Adam — after deslop

On the RIGHT panel, make a highly detailed half-body portrait photo of this character — show the skin and hair in fine detail. Take the hair texture from @图片 3 and adapt it onto this character in @图片 1. Take the skin texture details from @图片 4 and adapt them to this character, but make the skin tone paler / whiter than @图片 1. Use @图片 6 as the texture for the black t-shirt. Use @图片 5 as the BACKGROUND texture. On the LEFT panel, add a full-length photo of the character - keep it the same.

Victor — Cast the Antagonist

What it does: Establishes Victor’s human appearance before creating his reusable video element.

For Victor I explored AI Cast options, then selected the face that fitted the role. The selected still becomes the starting point for his human video element.

Casting: male, mid-40s, tall, amber eyes, short wavy gray hair, beard, formal clothing.

Ron — Build the Boss

What it does: generates the source character design in Soul Cinema. Use the approved result to create @char_ron_video_v1.

A three-panel character sheet of a fat-cat gangster boss on a plain solid seamless neutral grey background, evenly and neutrally lit. LEFT panel: full-length front view, head to shoes, standing calmly. CENTER panel: full-length back view of the same figure and outfit. RIGHT panel: a large close-up of his face looking straight into camera with a very serious, hard, unsmiling expression. A heavy, wealthy man with slicked-back hair, in a white shirt with the top button undone, an expensive watch and a thin chain.',

'STYLE': {

'Artistic Medium': 'photorealistic cinematic movie still, character design sheet',

'Aesthetic Movement / Genre': 'neo-noir crime drama character design, gangster-boss antagonist',

'Cultural / Historical Influence': 'modern organized-crime kingpin, crime-cinema register'

},

'COMPOSITION': {

'Framing': 'three equal vertical panels side by side, two full-figure and one face close-up',

'Perspective': 'straight-on eye-level, static, front and rear elevations plus face close-up, no distortion'

},

'SCENE': {

'Subject Characteristics': 'a heavy, overweight, wealthy man in his late 40s to 50s, a gangster-organization boss — big round belly, thick neck, jowly fleshy face, heavy brow; dark hair slicked straight back from the forehead, glossy and combed tight with pomade, sleek and neat, a little grey at the temples; a very serious, grave, hard expression — mouth firmly closed and set in a straight line, no smile, no smirk, jaw tight, brow low and heavy, cold steady unblinking eyes locked directly on the camera, stern, humorless, deadly serious authority; wardrobe: a crisp white dress shirt with the top button undone and collar open, sleeves cuffed, dark tailored trousers with a leather belt, polished shoes; an expensive luxury gold watch on his wrist and a thin gold chain visible at his open collar; grave, calculating, dangerous fat-cat presence; the back view shows the broad back in the white shirt, the belt and the slicked hair',

},

Turn the Approved Cast into Video Elements

Create a video element from each approved character image. Add the voice reference, then direct a short performance that shows the face moving and the character speaking. In this project, I recorded Adam’s vocal sounds myself and asked friends to record Ron and Victor. The goal was a living performance reference, not a different identity in every shot.

Save the results as @char_adam_video_v1, @char_ron_video_v1, and @char_victor_video_v1.

Adam Video Element — Recreation Template

What it does: Turns Adam’s approved image and voice recording into a reusable video performance reference.

Adam — video character sheet

Animate the attached approved Adam character image into a short video reference. Preserve his exact face, white hair, eyes, build, black leather outfit and silver accessories. Show a clear three-quarter face view, a small natural head turn, a blink, a quiet breath and restrained jaw movement. Use the attached authorized voice recording for his vocal performance; keep the face clearly readable. Neutral studio background, stable light, no camera orbit, no outfit changes, no music or on-screen text. Save the approved result as @char_adam_video_v1.

Victor Video Element — Recreation Template

What it does: Turns Victor’s approved human image and voice recording into a reusable video performance reference.

Viktor - video char sheet

Animate the attached approved human Victor character image into a short video reference. Preserve his exact face, gray hair, beard, build and formal wardrobe. Show a clear three-quarter face view, a small turn, a restrained knowing smile and a brief shift to irritation. Use the attached authorized voice recording for his vocal performance. Human appearance throughout; no claws, fangs, wound or transformation. Stable neutral light and background, no music or on-screen text. Save the approved result as @char_victor_video_v1.

Ron Video Element — Recreation Template

What it does: Turns Ron’s approved image and voice recording into a reusable video performance reference.

Ron — video character sheet

Animate the attached approved Ron character image into a short video reference. Preserve his exact face, gray hair, heavyset build and wardrobe. Show a clear three-quarter face view, a natural glance, a controlled exhale and a brief change from composure to irritation. Use the attached authorized voice recording for his vocal performance. Keep the mouth and eyes readable, with stable neutral lighting and an uncluttered background. No changes to identity, no added props, music or on-screen text. Save the approved result as @char_ron_video_v1.

Locations — Give Every Scene a Stable Space

Start with clean, empty environment plates. The reference supplies the layout, furniture, materials, and light. The scene prompt supplies the camera and action.

The Alley — @loc_alley_v1

What it does: creates the night alley and phone booth for Adam’s side of the call. The three-quarter angle gives the frame depth.

Wide establishing plate of a dark back street at night, deep neo-noir — a wide alley broad enough for cars to drive through, a two-lane stretch of wet asphalt running between the buildings. An old weathered telephone booth stands against the wall midway down the street, its glass panels scuffed and grimy, only the faintest dim glow barely visible inside. Brick and concrete walls line both sides, rusted fire escapes zig-zagging up the buildings, a dumpster and stacked trash bags against the wall, tangled cables overhead, a few faded painted lane marks on the road. Light wisps of steam drift lazily up from manhole covers and a street grate. The whole scene stays dark and moody, but a soft, low ambient light fills every part of the frame — a gentle diffuse city-glow spill from the sky and distant windows, faint bounce off the wet asphalt — so that even the shadowed corners, far end and walls retain readable detail and texture, with no fully black crushed areas anywhere. Wet asphalt and puddles reflect a single flickering security lamp and the dim ambient glow. Gritty, moody, dangerous, cinematic noir atmosphere.




CAMERA: wide 24mm anamorphic lens, three-quarter angle looking diagonally down the wide street toward the telephone booth, shallow depth with soft background blur and pronounced anamorphic oval bokeh on the distant lamp, strong anamorphic 24mm edge falloff with pronounced soft blur and horizontal streaking toward the left and right frame edges from field curvature, the center sharp while the corners smear into painterly anamorphic bloom, subtle horizontal blue streak lens flares, micro-handheld with natural operator-breathing sway.




STYLE: shot on real 35mm film, Kodak Vision3 500T grain; 24mm anamorphic lens with subtle horizontal flare, gentle highlight bloom and characteristic edge distortion and corner blur; rule-of-thirds composition; natural true-to-life color; realistic environment physics — light steam rising from the manholes, drifting haze, dripping water, blowing litter, flickering light; gritty documentary film realism, indistinguishable from a real photograph, absolutely no CGI or render look. Dark low-key noir night lighting with lifted shadows — a soft even ambient fill keeping detail visible throughout, blacks lifted and never crushed, a barely-there glow in the phone booth and a faint flickering practical. No cloned or duplicated elements. Clean environment plate, no characters, no cars. No text, no watermark, no logo. Aspect ratio 2.39:1.

Ron’s Office — @loc_room_ron_v1

What it does: Dark wood, a substantial desk, and a leather chair establish Ron’s space. Keep the approved room reference for both the opening call and the finale.

Ron’s office — final

Cinematic film still, three-quarter angle medium-wide shot of a private study at night, camera set at about seated eye level to one side of the room, looking diagonally across the desk toward the far corner. A classic wood-panelled study: a large heavy desk of dark polished walnut with a leather writing inlay, brass drawer pulls and a slightly worn edge, angled so its front corner is nearest the camera; behind it a tall high-backed leather armchair in deep oxblood, the leather creased and softly shining, turned a little toward the desk; along the back wall a tall glass-fronted wooden bookcase cabinet of the same dark wood, its shelves lined with old cloth- and leather-bound books, a few small framed pictures and objects; on the desk a green-shaded brass banker's lamp switched ON, a stack of papers, a fountain pen, a heavy glass tumbler, a closed leather folder. Behind the armchair in the far corner a tall brass floor lamp with a warm cream fabric shade, switched ON, its light spilling down the wall and across the back of the chair. Dark wooden floorboards with a worn Persian rug in deep reds, wood-panelled walls, a curtained window with heavy drapes drawn shut, a small side table with a decanter. Warm evening light only from the two lamps — the low golden pool of the banker's lamp on the desk and papers, the softer amber wash of the floor lamp behind the chair haloing its silhouette, gradual falloff into deep warm-brown shadow in the corners and along the ceiling, practical lamps blooming gently. Polished walnut, creased leather, brass, old paper, wool rug. Empty and still.




CAMERA: 35mm anamorphic lens, three-quarter angle at seated eye level, moderate depth of field with the desk corner, lamp and armchair sharp and the bookcase and far wall softening gently into anamorphic oval bokeh around the lamp glows, mild anamorphic edge character and a subtle horizontal warm flare from the floor lamp, micro-handheld sway.




STYLE: shot on real 35mm film with fine natural grain and filmic highlight roll-off; anamorphic lens character; warm low-key palette — deep brown-black shadows that retain texture, rich separated midtones of walnut, oxblood leather, brass and cream, the amber lamp light kept close to its sources without a uniform yellow filter, an articulate midtone range so wood, leather, paper, brass and rug each keep their own value; realistic environment physics — still air, faint dust in the lamp glow; gritty documentary film realism, indistinguishable from a real photograph, absolutely no CGI or render look. Night, warm, quiet, private.




Clean environment plate, no people, no silhouettes, no faces, no figures in the chair or reflected in the glass. No readable text on papers or book spines, no watermark, no logo. Aspect ratio 2.39:1.

The Club — @loc_club_dancefloor_v1

What it does: The club establishes the environment before crowd action is added. The referenced dancefloor asset used by the supplied scene nodes is shown separately from the location-development result.

Club — option 4 

Cinematic film still, three-quarter angle wide shot of a huge empty nightclub main room at night, camera set at about head height at the edge of the dancefloor to one side, looking diagonally across the empty floor toward the DJ booth, with the long bar visible in the far distance along the opposite wall. A cavernous industrial hall in a dark noir mood — a vast open floor of black polished concrete reflecting the few lights, a high dark ceiling of exposed steel trusses and rigging with the moving-head lights switched off and hanging dead, massive concrete columns rising into shadow, a raised DJ booth on a black stage at the far end with a wide empty console and a dark switched-off LED wall behind it, a VIP mezzanine along one side behind glass railings lost in darkness, and far away across the room the long BAR — dark wood and black stone backed by a floor-to-ceiling mirror wall and a dimly lit glass display of bottles, the only steady light in the room, a row of empty stools in front of it. Light: almost everything dark — a few work lights and a single narrow cold white spotlight left on above the DJ booth cutting a hard shaft through thin residual haze onto the empty stage, the low amber glow of the bar display in the far distance reflected in the mirror and along the polished floor, small red standby LEDs on the rig, everything else falling into deep black shadow with hard-edged pools of light, strong contrast, long shadows from the columns. Scattered plastic cups and confetti on the floor, an overturned stool, a coiled cable. Black concrete, steel, dark wood, glass, haze. Empty and silent.




CAMERA: wide 24mm anamorphic lens, three-quarter angle at head height from the edge of the floor, STRONG shallow depth of field — only the nearest stretch of polished floor, a cup and the base of the nearest column in focus, everything beyond melting fast into heavy anamorphic blur: the DJ booth and its spotlight dissolving into a soft stretched shaft and oval bloom, the distant bar reduced to a smeared band of amber light and stretched oval bokeh from the bottle display, the columns softening into dark vertical masses; dense anamorphic character — a long horizontal blue-white flare from the spotlight, pronounced edge softness and oval highlights, micro-handheld sway.




STYLE: shot on real 35mm film with fine natural grain and filmic highlight roll-off; anamorphic lens character; noir low-key palette — deep blue-black shadows that retain texture, hard cold white spotlight, contained warm amber from the bar held near its source, desaturated midtones of concrete and steel, high contrast, no uniform teal-and-orange wash, no neon signage anywhere; realistic environment physics — thin drifting haze in the spotlight, still air, reflections on the polished floor; gritty documentary film realism, indistinguishable from a real photograph, absolutely no CGI or render look. Night, after hours, hollow, ominous.




Clean environment plate, no people, no silhouettes, no faces, no figures on the floor, at the bar, in the booth or reflected in the mirror. No readable text, no watermark, no logo. Aspect ratio 2.39:1.

More Scene Elements

What it does: Keeps the motorcycle, locations, DJ, and pistol consistent across scenes.

Use these references to keep the motorcycle, locations, DJ, and pistol consistent.

Stage 2 — Direct the Scenes
Scene 1.1 — The Phone Call

What it does: Sets the job in motion: Ron loses his temper, Adam hangs up mid-word, then rides away.

One prompt, four useful takes. Keep Adam’s opening, Ron’s performance, the hang-up, and the departure, then combine them in the edit.

0:00 / 0:30
0:00 / 0:30
0:00 / 0:30
0:00 / 0:30

Opening — retain Adam’s first shot

SCENE CONTEXT

Block 1 of 6. Thirty seconds, 21:9. One call already in progress across two spaces. Ron has lost one of his own and is sending Adam after the killer. He starts holding himself together and comes apart in stages—nerves, simmer, boil, break. Adam listens, takes what he needs from the call, hangs up, and leaves. Gritty neo-noir, night.

ACTIVE REFERENCES

@char_ron_video_v1 — VIDEO ELEMENT — RON. Face, grey hair, hands, and wardrobe match the reference. VOICE: use the voice heard in this reference—the same timbre, low rough register, rasp, and breath—through every line, from nervous speech to the full scream. The scream is that same voice pushed to breaking. Ron holds a cigarette in his LEFT hand and a black corded receiver in his RIGHT hand. The reference controls identity and voice; pose, props, and lighting come from this prompt.

@char_adam_video_v1 — VIDEO ELEMENT — ADAM. Build, jacket, thin silver chain at the collar, and hands match the reference. Adam is seen only as a silhouette from behind, a hand, and a back. His face stays out of every frame. The reference controls appearance; Adam remains silent.

@loc_room_ron_v1 — RON’S OFFICE. Controls layout, materials, and practical lighting: a heavy wooden table, a warm table lamp, a glass ashtray, and a black landline base with a coiled cord. The reference’s camera angle and framing are not inherited.

@loc_alley_v1 — THE ALLEY. Controls layout and materials: wet asphalt, brick walls, a weak sodium lamp in the distance, and an old glass-and-steel booth against the left wall. Inside the booth is a smooth matte-black wall-mounted box with a steel cradle hook and a heavy black receiver on a cord. The reference’s camera angle and framing are not inherited.

@prop_bike — ADAM’S MOTORCYCLE with rear case. Controls the bike’s appearance.

LOCATION MAP

Room: the table runs left–right across the frame. Ron sits at the near long side, body turned three-quarters toward camera-right. The lamp is at the left end of the table; the ashtray and landline base are within reach. The camera works from Ron’s right side throughout.

Alley: a narrow corridor running away from the camera toward the distant sodium lamp. The booth stands against the left wall in the near depth, its doorway open toward the alley. @prop_bike is parked against the same wall about three metres beyond the booth, pointing down the alley. The camera works from behind and camera-right of Adam. His direction of travel is away from the camera into depth.

FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING

Open in Ron’s office. Extreme close-up of Ron’s mouth, jaw, and the receiver, viewed three-quarters from his right, with the camera rolled 15°. The receiver is already at his right ear, its lower mouthpiece beside his lips. He is already speaking.

Adam is already inside the booth in the alley, facing the wall-mounted box, with the receiver at his right ear and a cigarette in his left hand. His back faces the alley. The bike is parked farther along the wall. Exactly one person is present in each location.

FORMAT MODE

Controlled multi-shot sequence, 30 seconds, hard cuts. Each segment below specifies its opening frame.

OPTICS

Close-ups: 47° diagonal field of view, with a natural perspective.

Table medium-wide: 63° diagonal field of view.

Final alley wide: 84° diagonal field of view, classic wide rectilinear perspective.

Keep the lens fixed within each shot. Gentle horizontal flares may appear around practical lights.

CAMERA

Dialogue and stillness use a tripod or one slow, controlled move. Ron’s outburst is filmed on a locked-off tripod so his physical movement drives the shot. Adam’s departure uses a static, low camera, with the bike moving away into depth.

ACTION TIMING

0–5s — SHOT 1 — @loc_room_ron_v1.

Extreme close-up from just below Ron’s mouth, three-quarters from his right. Roll the camera 15° so the lamp light cuts diagonally across the frame. Show his mouth, jaw edge, and receiver against his right cheek. His lips are dry. He speaks quickly, with an unsteady breath between words, trying to sound in control.

RON: “This goddamn puppy need to learn its own place.”

His cigarette hand briefly enters the edge of the frame. Hard cut.

5–9s — SHOT 2 — @loc_alley_v1.

Tight close-up from behind Adam. Place the camera about 0.3 metres above the wet ground, just inside the open booth doorway and slightly to his right, aimed steeply up his back. Roll the camera 15°.

His dark shoulders, jacket collar, and receiver at his right ear fill the frame. A small glint catches the chain at his collar. The booth’s steel frame and glass close the shot on both sides, with only a sliver of wet street visible below.

Slowly push in toward his shoulders. His cigarette stays low in his left hand; a little smoke curls past his shoulder. His face remains hidden.

Through the receiver, Ron speaks lower, almost to himself. His voice is thin and filtered but recognisably the voice from @char_ron_video_v1.

RON: “I knew the things would go south…”

A swallow from Ron. Adam stays still. Hard cut.

9–12s — SHOT 3 — @loc_room_ron_v1.

Level medium-wide shot on a tripod, from Ron’s right-side three-quarter angle. The table runs across the lower frame, lamp on the left, ashtray and landline base within reach.

Ron leans back with the receiver at his ear. His cigarette hand jabs the air twice, then drops to the table. His fingers drum once, stop, and spread flat. He is trying to contain the anger.

Then he sits forward in one motion, chest thrust toward the desk, mouth wide. He screams the name at full voice.

RON: “NOW THEY KILLED KENNY!”

His voice tears on “Kenny.” A few drops of saliva catch the lamp light.

12–16s — SHOT 3 CONTINUES.

Ron rises out of the chair and leans over the table. He moves the receiver away from his ear and holds it upright in front of his face: the earpiece points upward, and the lower mouthpiece sits a few centimetres from his lips.

He shouts directly into that lower mouthpiece. The cord pulls taut. His left fist, still holding the cigarette, hits the wood on the final word. The ashtray jumps and spins, ash scatters, the lampshade shivers, and the cigarette snaps in his fingers.

RON: “I NEED THIS DOG'S HEAD ON MY TABLE!!!”

His voice is hoarse and cracks on “table.” A little saliva catches on his lower lip and the mouthpiece. He stays bent over the desk, breathing hard through his nose.

Hard cut as the next line begins.

16–18s — SHOT 4 — @loc_alley_v1.

Extreme close-up from behind and camera-right of Adam, at a three-quarter angle. Frame his right hand, the heavy black receiver, and the steel cradle hook directly below it. These fill the frame. Everything behind them is dark matte black and out of focus. His shoulder cuts the edge of the shot; his head remains above frame.

From the receiver, tiny and distorted:

RON: “This piece of sh—”

Adam slams the receiver onto the hook mid-word. One hard metal clack. Ron’s voice stops immediately.

Cut on the clack.

18–20s — SHOT 5 — @loc_alley_v1.

Close shot from behind and camera-right, at a low three-quarter angle beside @prop_bike. Adam’s back and the seat fill the frame. His cigarette has been discarded before he mounts the bike.

He is already swinging his leg over as the shot begins and drops onto the seat. The suspension compresses, his jacket pulls across his shoulders, and the chain shifts at his collar. His face stays hidden. No pause.

20–23s — SHOT 6 — @loc_alley_v1.

Low three-quarter close-up of Adam’s right hand from behind and camera-right of @prop_bike.

His hand closes around the throttle. His thumb hits the starter; the engine catches with a hard bark that echoes off the brick. He wrenches the throttle wide open. The engine roars, the bike shakes on its suspension, and exhaust smoke rolls across the lens.

23–30s — SHOT 7 — @loc_alley_v1.

84° wide shot from behind and camera-right of the bike, at a three-quarter angle. Keep the camera low, near knee height, beside the wall. Adam’s back faces the lens.

He drops the clutch and launches down the alley. The rear tyre spins on the wet asphalt, throwing water and grit. The suspension reacts to the acceleration. A short cloud of exhaust hangs where he was as his tail light shrinks toward the distant sodium lamp.

Hold on the empty alley: the engine fades, smoke thins over wet asphalt, and the dark booth remains on frame-left.

ACTING TASK — RON

The performance is carried by his voice, mouth, hands, and changing posture.

SCENE DIRECTION, UNSPOKEN:

Make Adam understand that this is personal.

MOTIVE:

Kenny was one of his own. Ron saw the danger coming and did nothing. Guilt is feeding the anger.

GOAL:

Get Adam moving tonight to bring him the killer’s head.

OBSTACLE:

His composure is failing. He needs to give a clear order, but guilt and rage keep breaking through.

TACTIC:

He talks quickly to cover the tremor in his voice. “I knew…” slips out as a confession to himself. He tries to contain the emotion through small movements: a jab of the hand, fingers drumming, a palm pressed flat against the table.

Kenny’s name breaks that restraint. From there, Ron uses volume and physical force to make the order land: he leans forward, brings the mouthpiece close, and hits the desk. He does not know Adam is about to cut him off.

MOMENT TO MOMENT:

“This goddamn puppy…” — fast, nervous, breath between words; trying to sound in control.

“I knew…” — quieter, slower, almost to himself; guilt surfaces.

The jabs and finger drumming — the last attempt to contain it.

“NOW THEY KILLED KENNY!” — screamed, with the voice tearing on the name.

The lunge — receiver raised to his mouth, fist on the desk.

“I NEED THIS DOG'S HEAD ON MY TABLE!!!” — the same voice pushed into a hoarse, exhausted scream.

“This piece of sh—” — pure anger, interrupted before he can finish.

Keep his eyes engaged, with natural blinking and responsive facial movement.

ACTING TASK — ADAM

Silent throughout. The performance is carried by stillness, his hands, and decisive movement.

GOAL:

Take what he needs from the call and leave to carry out the job.

OBSTACLE:

Ron’s escalating outburst makes it harder to get a clear instruction and end the conversation.

TACTIC:

Adam stays still and listens. Once he has what he needs, he stops giving Ron his time. He hangs up mid-insult without hesitation. Everything afterward flows into his departure: mount, starter, throttle, leave.

His face remains hidden throughout.

PHYSICS

Cigarette smoke curls with the room air and shifts with Ron’s breath. The ember brightens on an inhale. The receiver cord tightens and moves when he leans forward. The ashtray jumps, spins, and settles after the impact; ash scatters.

In the booth, smoke drifts past Adam’s shoulder. The receiver has weight and makes a hard clack against the hook.

The bike’s suspension compresses under Adam’s weight and responds to acceleration. The rear tyre throws water and grit from the wet road. Exhaust smoke bursts out, hangs briefly, then drifts and thins.

LIGHTING

Room: warm practical light from the table lamp at frame-left, with the room’s other practicals providing subdued background illumination. Ron’s right side falls into shadow, but his eyes remain readable in the medium-wide.

Alley: the distant sodium lamp provides the main backlight. Low ambient spill preserves the street’s depth while Adam remains a dark silhouette. His chain and wet surfaces catch small highlights. Keep his face hidden.

AUDIO

English only. Ron delivers the five specified lines exactly as written, all using the voice from @char_ron_video_v1. Preserve its timbre, register, and rasp through both the quiet lines and the screams.

Shot 1: Ron’s voice is heard directly in the room.

Shot 2: his voice is heard through Adam’s receiver, thin and filtered.

Shot 3: his outburst fills the room.

Shot 4: his final line is filtered through the receiver and stops instantly on the hang-up.

Adam never speaks. No music, narration, or subtitles.

Room sounds: room tone, cigarette inhale and exhale, fingers drumming on wood, the receiver cord pulling taut, a fist hitting the desk, and the ashtray ringing and spinning.

Alley sounds: water dripping, distant city hum, the receiver’s metal clack, jacket leather, suspension compression, the starter, engine noise echoing off brick, hard revving, water and grit thrown by the tyre, and the engine fading into the distance.

STYLE

Gritty neo-noir. Natural film grain, deep blacks, desaturated warm tungsten and sodium against cold steel. Anamorphic character, with oval bokeh and gentle horizontal flares around practical lights.

QUALITY

Readable faces and hands at held moments. Natural motion blur on fast movements, including Ron’s lunge, the desk impact, and the bike’s departure. Stable temporal continuity and consistent anatomy.

POSITIVE CONSTRAINTS

Exactly two characters: @char_ron_video_v1 in @loc_room_ron_v1 and @char_adam_video_v1 in @loc_alley_v1.

Adam appears only from behind, as a silhouette, or in close-ups of his hands and back. His face remains hidden in every shot.

Ron holds the cigarette in his LEFT hand and the receiver in his RIGHT hand throughout the office shots.

Adam holds his cigarette in his LEFT hand and the receiver in his RIGHT hand while inside the booth. His hands are free before he mounts the bike.

The wall-mounted device is a smooth matte-black box. The hang-up shot isolates Adam’s hand, the receiver, and the steel hook.

The call is already in progress at the first frame. Ron’s final line is interrupted by one hang-up.

Adam rides away from the camera into the depth of the alley.

Scene 1.2 — The Bridge at Night

What it does: Connects the alley to the club. The camera tracks the bike, moves behind Adam, then rises to reveal the bridge and city.

Keep the sky black. The wide reveal leaves room for the film title.

SCENE CONTEXT

20 seconds, one continuous take. Deep night — the middle of the night, hours from any sunrise. @char_adam_video_v1 rides @prop_bike across a long steel truss bridge, @loc_bridge_v1, into the city. The take opens tight on him from the front three-quarter, low, wheels and coat in detail, his face never shown; the camera swings around behind him and pulls up and back into a wide shot of the whole bridge, the traffic and the city panorama beyond — a black night skyline of lit windows under a black sky, thin smoke haze hanging over the rooftops. Gritty neo-noir, dry road, practical light only.




ACTIVE REFERENCES

@char_adam_video_v1 — VIDEO ELEMENT — ADAM. Build, jacket, long dark coat, boots, gloves, thin silver chain 100% match the reference. His face is never shown in this take: the camera stays below the chin in the close-up and behind him afterwards. Reference controls identity of the body and clothing.

@prop_bike — THE BIKE. Frame, tank, wheels, tyres, exhaust, headlight 100% match the reference. Headlight on, tail light on. Appearance only.

@loc_bridge_v1 — THE BRIDGE. Controls structure and materials: the steel truss walls either side, the lane markings, the towers ahead, the sodium road lamps along the railings. Time of day and camera angle are not inherited from the reference: in this shot it is the dead of night.




LOCATION MAP

A long straight bridge, four lanes, running away from the camera toward the city. Right-hand traffic: Adam rides in the right-hand carriageway, cars ahead of him and behind him in his lanes, oncoming headlights in the two lanes to his left. Steel truss walls close in either side of the roadway; sodium road lamps every 30 m on both railings throw orange pools onto dry dark asphalt. Ahead, the bridge's two towers with red aviation lights on their tops; beyond them the city skyline — dense towers, many windows lit yellow-white, the rest of each building black — under a fully black sky. Black water below the bridge on both sides. A thin smoke haze drifts over the far rooftops, lit faintly from below by the city.




FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING

Low angle, camera 0.5 m off the road, travelling alongside at riding speed, ahead of the bike and to its right, aimed back at it front three-quarter. In frame: the front wheel and fork of @prop_bike large in the foreground, the headlight blazing, the tank, Adam's gloved hands on the bars, his boots on the pegs, the knees, the long coat whipping back off his legs in the wind. Frame top cuts at his chest — head and face above frame. Behind him, the truss wall streaks past in orange lamp pools and darkness. Exactly one rider in the shot.




FORMAT MODE

Single continuous take, 20 seconds.




OPTICS

47° diagonal FOV, standard normal character, locked for the whole take. Focus on the bike and rider in the close section, then deep focus once the camera is wide behind him.




CAMERA

Vehicle-mounted travelling shot at the bike's speed. Opens low, ahead-right, front three-quarter on the wheels and body. Sweeps smoothly right-to-left around the bike's rear — passing the exhaust and rear wheel — to settle directly behind him, low, close on his back and the coat. Then, in one continuous move, pulls back and rises steeply, climbing to about 12 m above the roadway and 25 m behind him, so the whole bridge — both truss walls, all four lanes of traffic, the two towers ahead and the full skyline above them — fills the frame, the rider a small figure with a red tail light in the middle of his lane. Holds the wide, still travelling forward at his speed.




ACTION TIMING




0–5s — Front three-quarter, low, close. The front wheel spins with visible spokes and a blur of dry tyre on asphalt, the headlight beam sweeping the lane ahead; gloved hands steady on the bars, one thumb shifting on the grip; boots on the pegs; the coat cracking back in the wind off his legs. The truss wall strobes past behind him: orange pool, black, orange pool, black. An oncoming car's headlights flare across the far side and are gone.




5–8s — The camera sweeps around the bike's rear — the hot exhaust, the spinning rear wheel, the red tail light — and lands low directly behind him: his back, shoulders and the coat filling the frame, the head above the top of frame, the road ahead lit only by his headlight and the lamp pools. He leans a fraction into a lane correction and rides straight.




8–14s — The camera pulls back and climbs. His back shrinks in the frame; the two truss walls open out either side; the lanes reveal themselves — two cars ahead of him in his carriageway, tail lights red; two oncoming cars in the left lanes, headlights white; a third car behind him overtaking on the inside. The towers rise into frame ahead with their red aviation lights, and behind them the black skyline climbs, window-lights stacked into the dark.




14–20s — Wide hold, camera high and travelling. The bridge stretches ahead in perspective, its lamp chain converging toward the towers; the rider is a small shape with a red tail light in his lane, the coat a flicker behind him; traffic flows both ways. The city stands across the full width of the frame, thousands of scattered lit windows in black towers, the smoke haze over the roofs catching a faint orange from the streets below, and above everything a solid black night sky with a scatter of stars. Hold as he rides on toward the towers.




ACTING TASK — ADAM (body only; the work is in the hands and the posture):

GOAL: get across the river and into the city before anyone knows he is coming.

TACTIC: no hurry that shows. He rides steady, upright, shoulders down, hands quiet on the bars; the only movement is the small lean into a correction and the coat doing what the wind tells it. The speed is in the world moving past him, not in him.




PHYSICS

Real riding speed, about 70 km/h: the wheels spin with spoke blur, the bike sits planted on dry asphalt, the coat and jacket snap and stream backward, hands grip and adjust. Lamp pools pass at a steady rhythm. Cars move at realistic relative speeds — the oncoming ones fast, the ones in his lanes gaining or falling back slowly. The camera moves with the smooth continuous feel of a vehicle rig: the sweep around the bike and the climb are one long motion with no stops.




LIGHTING

Deep night, the dead middle of the night. The sky is black from horizon to zenith — the same black at the skyline's edge as overhead, no lightening anywhere along the horizon, no colour band behind the buildings. Every light in the shot is a practical: the bike's headlight beam on the road ahead and its red tail light; the orange sodium lamps along the bridge railings throwing hard pools onto the asphalt and warm streaks along the steel; red and white car lights; the red aviation lights on the tower tops; and the city — black buildings pierced by thousands of small lit windows, yellow-white and cold-white, with a low orange glow from the streets rising into the smoke haze over the rooftops. Between the lamp pools the road and the truss walls fall to full black. Adam's back and the bike are lit only as they pass through the lamp pools, orange then black, orange then black. Deep shadows everywhere, blacks kept black.




AUDIO

No dialogue, no music. The bike's engine steady at speed, a low rolling roar with the wind tearing across it. Tyres humming on dry asphalt. The coat cracking in the wind. Oncoming cars whipping past with a rising-falling whoosh. The steel truss ringing faintly as traffic crosses. As the camera climbs, the engine thins and the wind and the wide low rumble of the bridge and the distant city take over.




STYLE

Gritty neo-noir. Heavy natural grain, deep crushed blacks, orange sodium against pure night dark, the city a field of small lights in a black mass under a black sky. Anamorphic character: horizontal flares off the headlight and lamps, oval bokeh on the distant window-lights, soft bloom on the tower beacons.




QUALITY

Wheels and coat sharp and stable in the close section; smooth continuous camera sweep and climb with no cuts; consistent traffic direction; the skyline and sky stable through the hold; stable temporal cadence, no ghosting, no duplicated limbs or wheels.




POSITIVE CONSTRAINTS

One continuous take, one rider, face never shown — camera below the chin in the close section and behind him for the rest. Deep night for the whole take: black sky from the skyline's edge to the top of frame, the horizon the same black as the zenith, every light in the shot a lamp, a headlight, a window or a beacon. Right-hand traffic: Adam in the right-hand carriageway, oncoming headlights in the left lanes. Dry road throughout. The camera opens low front three-quarter on the wheels and coat, sweeps around behind him, then pulls back and climbs in one motion into a wide shot of the whole bridge, the towers and the black lit city under a black sky, with thin smoke haze over the rooftops.

Scene 1.3 — Arriving at the Club

What it does: Follows Adam from his motorcycle arrival through the club entrance and down the dark corridor, ending as the dancefloor’s cold light reaches his face.

SCENE CONTEXT

Test shot, 30 seconds, one continuous take across two locations. Night, the pavement along the club wall a few metres from the entrance. A drunk man, back to camera, walks along the wall, stops, braces a hand on the brick and throws up at the kerb. From camera-right down the road Adam arrives on his bike, rolls past him, parks, kicks the stand down, dismounts and walks toward the club entrance through a crowd that stretches along the whole pavement, past a second drunk being carried out. The bouncer at the door is busy with other people and never looks at him. The camera follows him through the door without a cut — and at the threshold, inside the same take, it lunges forward along his right side and lands on his right profile, half a metre off his shoulder, and from that moment tracks beside him down the corridor at his pace. The corridor is long, wide, high and black: a dark floor with a dull sheen, dark walls that rise into darkness, and nothing lit inside it. A few people come the other way toward the exit and pass him on his far side. Behind him the bright doorway to the street shrinks; ahead of him a cold, pale, hazy light from the big hall at the far end slowly grows on the front of his face out of the black. In the last metres the cold haze lights his profile fully and he walks out of the corridor into the hall with the camera riding beside him through the opening. Gritty neo-noir, practical light only.

ACTIVE REFERENCES

@char_adam_video_v1 — VIDEO ELEMENT — ADAM. Face, build, jacket, boots, thin silver chain at the collar 100% match the reference. In this shot he is legs and boots, then a back, and from the corridor threshold to the end his face in strict right profile, from his right side. Reference controls identity only.

@loc_club_exterior_v1 — THE CLUB ENTRANCE. Controls layout, materials and practical light: the street and kerb in front, the pavement, the club wall running away from the door, the door with its light spilling out. The reference's camera angle is not inherited.

@prop_bike — ADAM'S MOTORCYCLE with rear case. Appearance only.

The corridor and the hall beyond it are built from the descriptions in this prompt.

LOCATION MAP

Exterior: the street runs left–right across the frame, the kerb and pavement behind it, the club wall beyond the pavement. The club door is in the wall toward camera-left, set back about six metres from the kerb. Camera-right of the door, about eight metres along the same wall: the stretch of pavement where the drunk walks and stops. Between the two, about four metres from the door along the kerb: the empty spot where Adam parks. The crowd occupies the pavement from the door outward in both directions along the wall — densest at the door, thinning to loose groups of two and three leaning on the wall and standing at the kerb thirty metres either way; the drunk is at the thin end of it. The bouncer stands just outside the doorway with the door light behind him, working the rope with the people in front of him. Camera stays on the street side of the action throughout the exterior.

Interior: through the door, a straight corridor about fifteen metres long, 4 metres wide and 5 metres high — a wide, tall, industrial passage. The floor is dark, sealed and smooth, with a dull sheen that holds a faint smeared reflection of the far light. The walls are dark grey-black — painted steel panels and dark render — with a steel handrail along one side; they rise into a ceiling lost in black. The corridor has no fixtures and nothing lit inside it. At the far end it opens through a wide, tall doorway onto the main hall, which is not seen as a room from inside the corridor — only its light comes through: a cold, pale, hazy light, grey-white with a touch of blue, steady and even, the smoke-filled light of a big hall seen from a black hallway. It is faint at the corridor's near end and fills the last few metres before the opening. Beyond the opening, in the final second: tall pale concrete columns rising into haze, a balcony line high on the far wall with small dark figures along it, a crowd below with hands raised, all of it soft and desaturated in the cold smoke, a single small warm flare of one light somewhere in the middle of it. Adam walks a straight line down the corridor about a metre right of its centre line. The camera, from the threshold on, rides on his right side between him and the right wall, half a metre off his right shoulder, aimed square across at his right profile, so that in the frame he faces and walks toward frame-left, the far light is off frame-left, and the street door recedes off frame-right behind him. The people leaving walk along his far side — his left — toward the door.

FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING

Camera in the road about 2.5 m out from the kerb, 0.6 m off the asphalt, aimed at the pavement from a three-quarter angle behind and camera-right of the drunk, rolled about 15° on its axis. The drunk is already in frame: a man in a rumpled shirt and jacket, back to camera, walking away from the lens along the wall toward the door, unsteady, one hand trailing on the brick, framed from the knees to the shoulders with his head above frame. Behind him, out of focus, the crowd along the wall as a soft band of legs and bodies, the club door glowing at the far end. Adam and the bike are not yet in frame.

FORMAT MODE

Single continuous take, 30 seconds, no cuts. The location change happens inside the take by walking through the door; the change of angle from behind him to his profile happens inside the take as one fast forward lunge at the threshold.

OPTICS

84° diagonal FOV, classic wide rectilinear character, locked for the whole take. Shallow focus at the start on the drunk's back and hand on the wall; focus rides with the bike, then with Adam's back, snaps onto his profile as the camera lands beside him, and holds on the profile to the end.

CAMERA

One continuous handheld-on-gimbal move in seven phases. Phase one: canted three-quarter push-in on the drunk, the roll slowly straightening as it closes. Phase two: pull back and swing right as the bike enters, picking it up and tracking left with it at low height. Phase three: drop to road level for the boots and the stand. Phase four: rise behind Adam to shoulder height as he stands. Phase five: follow behind him through the crowd to the door at shoulder height, directly behind him. Phase six — the lunge: as his boot crosses the threshold the camera accelerates hard straight forward along his right side, rising from shoulder height to head height as it goes, and in about a metre and a half of travel draws level with his head — half a metre off his right shoulder, 1.7 m high, turned 90° to its own direction of travel so that it looks square across at his right profile — and settles there, already tracking at his walking pace. Phase seven: tracking beside him down the corridor at his pace, locked on the right profile, the black wall sliding past far beyond him, through the wide opening at the far end and one step beyond it.

ACTION TIMING

0–4s — Canted three-quarter, rolled 15°, from behind and camera-right. The camera pushes straight in on the drunk as he weaves along the wall, back to lens, hand dragging on the brick. The roll slowly levels as it closes. Three steps in he stops, plants the hand flat on the wall, bends forward toward the kerb — still back and shoulder to camera, head down and above frame — and throws up onto the gutter: his back heaving twice, the splash hitting the wet asphalt at the kerb stone. The camera is close on his back and the hand on the brick when the second heave hits.

4–7s — Headlight glow sweeps across the wall and the drunk's legs from camera-right. The camera pulls back off the drunk and swings right toward the road, dropping to about 0.4 m, and picks up the front tyre of @prop_bike rolling in from camera-right, low and large. The camera turns with it and begins tracking left alongside at walking pace, the drunk sliding out of frame at the right edge, still bent over the kerb. In frame: the lower half of the bike — front wheel, fork, engine, footpeg — and Adam's boots and lower legs on the pegs.

7–11s — Tracking left at low height along the gutter. The bike slows and stops at the empty spot by the kerb. The camera drops to road level, about 0.2 m, close on the pegs: Adam's left boot comes off the peg and finds the asphalt; his right boot flicks the side stand down with a metal click, close-up on the stand hitting the ground; the bike leans onto it and the suspension settles. The engine cuts. His legs swing off the seat.

11–14s — The camera rises smoothly from road level to about 1.2 m as he stands, swinging in behind him and to camera-right so it sees his back: jacket, shoulders, the chain at the back of his collar. He steps up onto the kerb and walks toward the club door without looking back. The camera falls in behind him at shoulder height, his back filling the centre of frame. Along the pavement on both sides of his path the crowd is already there: people leaning on the wall, smoking, a couple pressed together, a group of four laughing, others at the kerb looking at their phones, bodies in club clothes lining the whole wall.

14–19s — Following behind him, the camera moves with Adam into the thick of the crowd in front of the door: twenty or more people, backs and shoulders mostly toward the door, a few turning to look at him as he passes. From camera-left of the door two bouncers in dark suits come out carrying a man between them, one under each arm, his head lolling, feet dragging on the pavement; they haul him past Adam toward the kerb and dump him sitting against the wall as Adam keeps walking. Ahead, the club door with warm light spilling out and one large bouncer at the rope, turned camera-left toward a group of three arguing with him: he has a flat hand up to them, shaking his head, then jabs a finger at one of them and keeps talking. Through the open doorway behind the bouncer the corridor is black — a tall black opening — with only a faint cold pale haze far down at its end. Adam walks past the bouncer's shoulder on the right side and steps through the open doorway, back to camera; the bouncer's head stays turned to the group the whole time.

19–20.5s — The lunge. The instant Adam's boot lands inside the threshold the camera accelerates hard straight forward along his right side — his right shoulder and the right door jamb smearing past, the black wall streaking on the right of the picture — and in a metre and a half draws level with his head, turning square to him as it arrives, and lands: half a metre off his right shoulder, 1.7 m high, aimed straight across at his right profile, already moving with him. The frame never breaks — one fast smear forward that resolves into the profile. What resolves is @char_adam_video_v1 in strict right profile, for the first time: brow, nose, the line of the mouth, the jaw, the ear, the chain at the collar — a dark profile, the features barely there, edged from behind (frame-right) by the last of the warm door light on the back of his head and shoulder, and touched from ahead (frame-left) by a first faint grey of the far light on the brow and the tip of the nose. Beyond him, frame-left and behind him, the bright warm rectangle of the street door with the soft shapes of the crowd inside it, already sliding away frame-right. Around him the corridor is black. He faces frame-left and walks frame-left, eyes on the far opening, and gives the camera nothing.

20.5–27s — Tracking beside him at his pace, the profile locked in the centre of the frame, his face toward frame-left. With each of his steps the bright doorway behind him shrinks and slides off frame-right, and the cold pale light from ahead reaches further onto the front of his face: first a faint grey-white on the brow and the bridge of the nose, then the cheekbone, the upper lip, the line of the jaw, the profile slowly resolving out of the dark in cool desaturated tones, the black wall far beyond him taking a faint grey from it, evenly, step by step, the way a hall's light reaches down a long passage. Nothing switches on; nothing flickers; the light from ahead is steady and grows only because he walks toward it. The dark floor under his boots carries a faint smeared reflection of that light, brightening toward frame-left. The people leaving come out of the pale light on his far side — his left, beyond him in the frame — and walk frame-right toward the door, passing behind his profile: at 22s a couple, arm in arm, the girl carrying her heels in her hand, soft grey shapes that go dark and then become silhouettes against the door behind him; at 24s a man alone, jacket over his shoulder, checking his phone, the phone a small bright point moving frame-right past his shoulder; at 25.5s two guys laughing, one steadying the other, drifting toward the far wall as they go. Adam does not shift for any of them; they are beyond him in the wide corridor. His pace does not change. His eyes stay ahead, frame-left, on the opening.

27–30s — The last three metres. The cold pale light is full on the front of his profile now — soft, even, grey-white with a touch of blue, brow to chin lit, the back of his head dark, a faint haze in the air around him; behind him, frame-right, the street door is a small bright rectangle at the far end of a tall black tunnel. The tall right-hand jamb of the wide opening sweeps through the frame from left to right as camera and man pass it together, and beyond him the hall opens: tall pale columns rising into smoke, the balcony line with its small dark figures, a blur of raised hands, one small warm flare of a light deep in it, all soft and desaturated in the cold haze. He crosses the threshold and walks out into the hall in profile, eyes still ahead, the camera riding beside him, the space beyond him a soft blur of cold light and smoke with no readable faces. The take ends one step past the threshold, his profile centred, still walking, the pale light full on his face.

ACTING TASK — ADAM (silent; legs, then back, then profile; the work is in the walk and, from the threshold on, in the eyes):

GOAL: get to the light at the end of that corridor and through it.

OBSTACLE: nothing he acknowledges — the drunk at the kerb, the crowd, the man being dragged past, the bouncer, the camera landing at his shoulder, the people coming the other way, the dark are all in his way and none of them registers.

TACTIC: he does not slow for anything. He rolls past the vomiting man without a flicker, the stand goes down in one flick, he is off the bike and walking before the engine has finished dying, he walks past the bouncer as if the bouncer is furniture, and he walks into the black corridor without a pause to let his eyes adjust — straight down the corridor toward the pale light. When the camera lands beside him he gives it nothing: his eyes are on the opening ahead, jaw set, the same pace, the head never turning toward the lens. The face is shown because the camera moved, not because he did. The people leaving are on his far side and he does not look at them. The pace never changes from kerb to hall.

Moment to moment:

— the lunge — the camera arrives at his shoulder; his eyes stay on the opening; the profile does not turn

— the first steps — a dark profile against black, edged by the door behind, eyes forward

— the passes — no shift, no glance

— the profile in the pale light — eyes on the opening, steady; the face is a man arriving somewhere he has been before

— the threshold — no change on the face as he crosses it

(Safety: eyes engaged forward on the light ahead — never toward the lens, never glassy or unfocused; natural blink cadence.)

ACTING TASK — THE DRUNK AT THE WALL (back to camera throughout; legs, back and hand only):

GOAL: not fall down.

TACTIC: the wall is the only thing holding him up; every movement is about keeping the hand on the brick. The heave comes from the gut and bends the whole back; afterwards he does not move, because moving means the next one.

ACTING TASK — THE DOOR BOUNCER (background, soft focus):

GOAL: get this group of three to leave the rope.

TACTIC: they are his whole night right now — flat hand, head shake, the pointed finger, talking over them. Adam passing a metre to his right is not an event; he does not turn his head, because the three in front of him are the problem and the man walking in is not.

ACTING TASK — THE PEOPLE LEAVING (three brief passes, all on Adam's far side):

Each of them is done for the night and heading for the street: the couple are wrapped up in each other and pass without looking; the man with the phone is reading and keeps to his own side of the wide corridor; the two guys are half-carrying each other and wander toward the far wall. None of them looks at Adam or at the camera.

PHYSICS

Drunk: weight on the hand against the wall, knees soft, the heave rocks the whole torso forward and back, liquid hits wet asphalt and spreads toward the gutter. Bike: rolls at walking pace, headlight beam slides across legs and wall, suspension settles as it leans onto the stand, engine vibration stops on cut. Boots: heel contact on asphalt, then kerb, then the hard dark corridor floor, then the threshold. Camera: handheld-on-gimbal weight in every move, small settle at the end of each phase; the lunge at the threshold is one hard straight acceleration forward with real motion smear, a rise of half a metre, a 90° turn of the lens toward him as it draws level, and a firm settle into the side-track, which then carries the faint bob of an operator walking beside him at a steady pace. Crowd outside: bodies shift and close behind Adam as he passes. Carried drunk: dead weight between the two bouncers, heels scraping the pavement, slumps when set down. Corridor: the light on Adam changes only because he walks — the door behind him shrinks, the cold light from ahead grows evenly on the front of his profile with distance walked; the dark floor returns a faint smeared reflection of the far light; the people leaving have real weight, heels in hand swinging, the drunk pair leaning into each other.

LIGHTING

Exterior, practical only: the bike headlight sweeping the wall, the drunk's legs and the gutter, then off; the club door's warm spill lighting the pavement, the crowd and the bouncer from behind; a cold streetlight off frame-right giving the wet asphalt its sheen and edging the crowd along the wall. Adam is backlit by the door light as he walks toward it, his back and shoulders rimmed, face turned away and unlit. Corridor: two sources and nothing else, and in profile they split him front and back. From behind him, frame-right: the street door — a warm bright rectangle that shrinks and slides away with every step, rimming the back of his head, the back of his shoulder and the chain, strongest in the first steps and gone by the middle of the corridor. From ahead of him, frame-left: the light of the hall through the wide opening — cold, pale, hazy, grey-white with a touch of blue, steady and even — lighting the front of his profile, brow to chin, faint at the near end so the features are barely there, growing evenly along the fifteen metres until by the last three it lights the whole front of the face softly and fully, cool and desaturated, the back of the head staying dark. Between the two sources the corridor is black: walls, ceiling and floor go to black, with a faint grey on the far wall and a faint reflection on the floor only where the far light reaches. The overall image inside is black with a warm rectangle receding frame-right and a cold pale light growing from frame-left. Past the threshold, the hall: cold hazy light through smoke, pale columns, one small warm flare deep in the room. No strobes, no hard flashes anywhere inside.

AUDIO

No dialogue, no music outside. Scuffing shoes and a hand dragging on brick, a wet retch, liquid on asphalt, ragged breathing. Engine approaching from the right, rolling at idle past him, wet tyre on asphalt. Side stand click close, engine cutting, boots on asphalt then kerb. Crowd murmur along the whole pavement, laughter, lighters, growing as he approaches the door; the bouncer's raised voice and the group of three talking over him, indistinct; club bass thudding through the door; the carried drunk groaning. Through the doorway the street cuts off and the corridor takes over: bass and kick drum from the far end, muffled, growing with every step, echoing long off the hard walls of the tall wide passage; boots on the dark floor with a slight echo; the passing couple's murmur, heels clicking in her hand, the drunk pair laughing, all fading behind him toward the door. As he crosses the threshold the full dance-floor mix hits — heavy four-on-the-floor beat, crowd roar — and swallows everything else.

STYLE

Gritty neo-noir. Heavy natural grain, deep blacks, desaturated warm door light and sodium against cold steel and wet asphalt outside; inside, black on black — a bright shrinking doorway behind him, black walls and a black ceiling, a faint reflection on a dark floor, and a cold pale grey-white haze growing from frame-left as the only light on the front of his profile; the palette inside is warm door-light, cold grey-white and black, and nothing else. Anamorphic character: oval bokeh, gentle horizontal flares off the headlight and the door light, soft bloom on the pale haze and on the door behind him.

QUALITY

Natural 180-degree shutter motion cadence: brief directional blur on the heave, the camera swing at the bike, the tyre, and heavy directional smear through the lunge at the threshold; sharp readable holds on the hand on the wall, the stand, the boots, Adam's back in the crowd and his profile from the settle to the end. The lunge reads as one continuous forward move with the profile resolving out of the smear. Smooth even exposure ramp on the front of the face along the corridor from near-black to fully lit; profile sharp, stable and locked in the centre of frame, eyes fixed ahead; stable temporal cadence, no ghosting, no duplicated limbs.

POSITIVE CONSTRAINTS

One continuous take across @loc_club_exterior_v1 and the entrance corridor described in this prompt, the change made by walking through the door. One drunk at the wall, one bike, one Adam. The drunk keeps his back to the camera for the whole take and is framed below the head. Adam is framed as legs on the bike, then as a back, and his face appears at 19–20.5s when the camera lunges straight forward along his right side at the corridor threshold and lands half a metre off his right shoulder in one continuous move; from that settle to the end of the take the camera tracks beside him at his pace and his face is in strict right profile, facing and walking toward frame-left, the street door receding frame-right behind him; his eyes stay ahead on the opening for the whole corridor and his head does not turn. The canted horizon lives only in the opening push-in and levels out before the bike enters. The crowd lines the pavement along the wall in both directions and is densest at the door; a second drunk is carried out by exactly two bouncers and set against the wall; the door bouncer faces the group of three on his left for the whole time Adam passes. The corridor is 4 metres wide, 5 metres high, fifteen metres long, with a dark smooth floor and dark grey-black walls rising into a black ceiling, and nothing lit inside it; its light is the street door behind him and the cold pale grey-white haze of the hall through the wide opening at the far end, faint near the door and growing evenly on the front of his profile as he walks toward it; the palette inside is warm door-light, cold grey-white and black. Exactly five people pass Adam heading for the exit, in three groups, all on his far side beyond him in the frame, walking frame-right toward the door. The take ends one step past the threshold with the camera beside him and the hall beyond him as cold hazy light, pale columns and soft unreadable figures. The bike passes the drunk without touching him.
