Scene 2.6 — The Club Fight

What it does: Makes a three-on-one fight readable. Adam deals with the guard behind him, then the right, then the senior guard on the left.

SCENE CONTEXT

Thirty-second multi-shot sequence, real time, twenty-one shots, cut like a fight montage. It opens on a crane shot that starts straight above Adam — a top shot into a packed dancing crowd with three black suits cutting three lines through the heads toward him from three sides — and sinks and swings down in one move until it ends on his face. Then the three guards reach him at once, from the left, from the right and from behind, and he takes them one at a time, fast, exact and sharp — a pivot, a pin, a duck, and short straight strikes that go out and back in a quarter of a second — until all three are on the wet concrete around his boots. He goes still, pushes his hair back with one hand, settles his coat, and walks out of frame into the dancers. The strobe fires every three seconds for the whole thirty, locked to the clock; a fire cannon every five.




ACTIVE REFERENCES

@char_adam_video_v1 — VIDEO ELEMENT — the man (Adam): face, platinum-blond tousled hair, light mustache, hoop earrings, silver medallion chain, black tee, long black leather coat to the ankles, black leather trousers with chain belt, leather cuff and silver rings on the right hand, black leather boots — all 100% match the reference; his eyes stay exactly as in the reference in colour, shape and size in every shot. Ignore the reference's studio background. He carries no weapon and never touches a baton in this block.

@char_club_guard_1_image_v1 — Guard 1, the senior: the biggest and heaviest of the three, a broad heavy face with a strong jaw and a hard flat stare, thick dark hair swept up and back to the side, black suit over a black shirt buttoned to the collar, black leather shoes — 100% matches the reference. Ignore the reference's white studio background and its three-view layout; he is one whole man in the club. He attacks from screen-left, last, and goes down last.

@char_club_guard_2_image_v1 — Guard 2: a lean East Asian man in his forties, narrow face with sharp hollow cheekbones, dark hair slicked straight back tight to the skull, thin mouth, black suit over a black shirt buttoned to the collar, black leather shoes — 100% matches the reference. Ignore the reference's white studio background and its three-view layout; the face is the one in the reference's left panel. He attacks from screen-right, second.

@char_club_guard_3_image_v1 — Guard 3, the youngest and fastest: a young East Asian man, smooth clean-shaven face, neat dark hair parted and combed back to the side, black suit over a black shirt buttoned to the collar, black leather shoes — 100% matches the reference. Ignore the reference's white studio background and its three-view layout; he is one whole man in the club. He attacks from behind, first, and goes down first.

@prop_electric_baton — one per guard, three in the block, identical and 100% matching the reference: about 55 cm long: a black ribbed rubber grip with a wrist-strap loop at the butt end and a round black hand guard at the front; on top of the grip just behind the guard, a small switch and a red indicator dot; the shaft a row of worn, scuffed chrome steel rings with thin black gaps between them; a black end cap with two short steel prongs sticking straight out of it. It is a contact weapon and a light rigid club. The reference shows it switched off — dark and silent, the rings dull steel — and that is how it is carried: a guard carries it DARK, low along the thigh, arm straight, prongs toward the floor, thumb resting on the switch, and presses the switch only in the instant he strikes — the sparks are born in the first hand's-width of the swing and die the moment the thumb lifts. The switch is a momentary switch: while it is held down the red indicator dot glows and bright blue-white sparks jump between the two prongs and race along the steel rings with a loud dry crackle; let go, and it is dark and silent again inside a frame. Thick leather does not conduct. The instant it leaves a hand it goes dark and silent. A baton is never carried live and never carried on the shoulder or against the body.

@loc_club_dancefloor_v1 — the club: tall concrete pillars on both sides, steel gallery across the far wall, raised DJ booth under two spotlights at the far end, two large fans high on the back wall, wet concrete floor, small warm practicals at the pillar bases, cold blue-grey haze. Controls geometry, materials and light only.




IDENTITY LOCK — THREE DIFFERENT MEN

Exactly four named men appear: @char_adam_video_v1 and the three guards. @char_club_guard_1_image_v1, @char_club_guard_2_image_v1 and @char_club_guard_3_image_v1 are three different real men, each with his own face exactly from his own reference image — never the same face twice, never merged, never replaced by a generic bouncer: the big heavy one with the broad face and thick swept-back hair, a head taller and much wider than the other two; the lean one in his forties with the sharp hollow cheekbones and hair slicked straight back; the young one with the smooth face and neat side-parted hair. Whenever a guard appears he keeps his reference face, even in motion blur, in the strobe frame and in close-up. Everyone else is an anonymous club-goer.




PLAYABLE INTENTIONS

Adam is standing inside the crowd, and three men are coming for him at once from three sides; that is the obstacle, and the tactic is the oldest one: do not fight three, fight one three times — move so that only one of them can reach him at a time, and finish each before the next arrives. Nothing he does is wide or angry: every answer is the shortest line, out and back, and between answers he is completely still. The guards want it quick and ugly and all at once; each one arrives a half-beat apart, and each one is alone when he gets there. The audience's read: three armed men were never a fight, and he knew it before they did. At the end he pushes his hair back — the one human, careless gesture in the block — and walks off as if he had been dancing. Adam's face shows only the jaw, the nostrils and the vein at his temple. The crowd wants its night and never stops dancing.




LOCATION MAP AND SCREEN DIRECTION

Long industrial hall (@loc_club_dancefloor_v1). Adam stands deep inside the packed dancing crowd on the dancefloor; bodies overlap him and there is no ring, no circle and no empty floor around him at any moment — the fight happens in a body-wide gap between dancers that opens and closes around the men, not in a clearing. The camera stays on the DJ side of Adam for the whole block after the crane. @char_club_guard_1_image_v1 comes at him from screen-left; @char_club_guard_2_image_v1 from screen-right; @char_club_guard_3_image_v1 from behind him, from deep in the frame. When Guard 3 swings from behind, Adam pivots a quarter turn to his right and the guard's own weight carries him past Adam toward the camera, so Guard 3 is fought and dropped screen-right of Adam and a step nearer the lens; Guard 2 then comes in from screen-right over his fallen partner's legs; Guard 1 comes in from screen-left. Adam faces three-quarters to the camera throughout. At the end he walks out of frame screen-right. The light is exactly the light of the location reference image, unchanged.




SHOW RHYTHM — LOCKED

The stroboscope fires at 120, 123, 126, 129, 132, 135, 138, 141, 144, 147 s — every 3 seconds on the clock without fail, ten flashes in thirty seconds, never faster, never a series, never a flicker, never a double flash. It is a xenon flash a fraction of a millisecond long — like a single camera flash going off — so exactly one frame is lit, never a lit second: the hall goes hard blue-white from the overhead truss, the haze glows white, the wet floor flashes like a mirror, every body and every drop and every spark frozen razor-sharp with no motion blur, hard black shadows thrown straight down so eye sockets and the undersides of chins go black; the very next frame the hall is back to its own dark light, a faint negative after-image hanging for a moment. The first frame of the sequence and the first frame of every shot is in the club's own dark light; a strobe never lands on the first frame of a shot — each flash is placed inside its shot below, and the decisive ones freeze: the three lines converging from above at 123, Adam's face with the three shapes at the edges at 126, the coat fanned on the pivot at 129, the pin-and-punch at 132, Guard 2 hitting the floor at 135, the fourth fist in the big man's mouth at 138, the three men down around his boots at 141, the hand in the hair at 144.

A fire cannon fires at 120, 125, 130, 135, 140, 145 s — every 5 seconds on the clock, one at a time, alternating left wall, right wall, far gallery, never in the crowd: a 2–3 m column of propane flame that launches straight up, rolls at the top and dies into a soot curl within a second, heat shimmer above the nozzle and gone; its orange light rolls across the nearest faces and is gone with it.

These two rhythms are locked for the whole sequence and, with the sparks of a baton during each swing, are the only additions to the location's own light.




THE CROWD — DANCING PACKED TIGHT, NEVER SCATTERING, NOT CARING

The crowd is ordinary club-goers in all kinds of different everyday and party clothes and colours — a white shirt, a red dress, a denim jacket, a yellow top, a striped tee, a green bomber, a silver sequin dress, a grey hoodie, a pink blouse, bare shoulders, a leather jacket, a baseball cap — young men and women of every look; only the guards wear black suits. Nobody in the crowd stands still for a single frame: they dance packed tight shoulder to shoulder, and every person moves hard in their own way — one girl whips her long hair in circles, a guy jumps straight up again and again, a couple grinds pressed together, a girl rolls her hips with her arms straight up, two friends bounce shoulder to shoulder, someone spins, someone drinks from a bottle mid-dance, someone films the DJ with a phone held high, arms go up in ragged waves. The crowd never forms a ring, a circle or an audience around anyone — nobody stands watching with a drink like a spectator, nobody faces the fight on purpose — it never scatters, and it does not care: they laugh, sing, jump, drink and dance, sway around three men on the floor without a glance, and let the guards and Adam squeeze between them without noticing. Nobody in the crowd is ever touched: no blow, no baton, no falling body reaches a club-goer — the dancers nearest a swing or a fall sway a half-step clear and dance on. The packed dancing crowd is visible in every shot, and nobody looks into the lens. Recurring faces: THE GIRL IN RED (short red dress, long dark hair) laughing with her arms up; THE BOYFRIEND (tall, white shirt) spinning a blonde girl in a yellow top; THE BOTTLE GUY (lanky, cap backwards, green bomber) jumping with his bottle high.




CAMERA LANGUAGE

Shot 1 is one continuous crane move: it starts aimed straight down at the floor from about 7 m directly above Adam, sinks and swings round in one smooth, even move, and ends at about 1.2 m from his face at a three-quarter angle, slightly above his eye line — top shot to close-up in one breath, no stops, no speed changes. From shot 2 on, every shot is raw handheld from an operator inside the crowd, bumped by dancers: hard shake, the horizon rocking up to ten degrees, whip pans to whatever moves fastest with focus catching up late. In the fight, shots 4–16, the shake is at its maximum: the frame jerks several times a second, smeared with motion blur, the horizon swinging up to fifteen degrees, as if the operator is being shoved inside the fight. IMPACT JOLT: at the exact frame each contact lands the whole frame kicks hard several degrees in the direction of the force, then shakes on. CAMERA CHASES THE BLOW: on every contact the operator lunges one or two steps in along its line and arrives tight on the point of contact at the instant it lands — the chin, the knee, the nose, the jaw, the neck, the mouth, the sternum — then is already moving to the next target. Every shot of Adam is a three-quarter angle; the top shot looks straight down on him with his shoulders turned at a three-quarter angle to the frame. The strobe does the rest of the style: the decisive frames are frozen razor-sharp for one frame and gone.




SPEED — FAST, EXACT, SHARP

Every strike Adam throws is a snap: it leaves guard, lands and is back inside a quarter of a second; the first visible motion of the limb IS the strike itself, launched from where the hand or foot already rests, with no draw-back and no wind-up; a combination is three to four snaps a second. Every strike names its limb, its striking surface and its exact target and lands exactly there at full speed with no glancing contact; the frame of contact is crisp — knuckles, elbow, heel or the bottom of the fist visibly pressed into the named spot — and the fist is already back at guard while the man is still moving from the hit. Between exchanges Adam is completely still in his narrow side-on stance: the fight is short straight lines and long stillness. Natural 180-degree shutter action cadence: fast strikes and evasions carry brief directional motion blur, then resolve into sharp readable impact poses; no slow motion, no ghosting, no duplicated limbs, no frame-skipping stutter.




FULL CONTACT — A REAL FIGHT

This is a real, brutal fight, not a staged dance. Every action makes full, solid contact: at the frame of impact the knuckles are visibly pressed into the chin, the nose, the mouth; the heel is visibly driven into the side of the knee and into the sternum; the point of the elbow visibly crosses the jaw; the bottom of the fist visibly lands on the back of the neck. Bodies deform at the point of contact, heads are driven along the line of the force, sweat and spit spray. Skin stays clean and unbroken the whole time — every impact shows only the dent, the snap, the sweat and the spit. No blood anywhere in the block. Each decisive contact gets its own tight insert.




MONTAGE

Cut like a real action montage: twenty-one hard cuts in thirty seconds. The opening crane holds for six seconds; every shot inside the fight is under one and a half seconds and most are under one; every other shot is two seconds or shorter. In the fight every cut lands on an impact or on the start of a move, and each decisive contact is shown twice — the move in a medium shot, then the hit in a tight insert — match-cut on the direction of the force. The three fights overlap by a half-beat: as one man goes down the next is already swinging. The block opens from above and ends with the crowd closing behind him.




FORMAT MODE

Anamorphic 2.39:1. Real time throughout, no slow motion anywhere. Hard cuts, no dissolves.




OPTICS

Anamorphic look, lens wide open: very shallow depth of field, only the subject's plane sharp, heavy creamy blur with horizontal oval anamorphic bokeh. 35mm (about 63° diagonal FOV) for the crane, full bodies, legs and the crowd, 50mm (about 47°) for medium shots, 85mm (about 29°) for faces, hands and inserts. Lens locked inside each shot.

PHYSICS

The crowd dances on and is never touched; every swing, fall and body stays inside the body-wide gap between dancers, who sway a half-step clear and dance on. Wet concrete: soles skid, bodies slide, a falling man splashes. A guard's baton is dark until the strike: the thumb presses the momentary switch as the swing starts, so the sparks are born in the first hand's-width of the swing and the crackle starts with it; the instant the fingers open it goes dark and it clatters and bounces once on its rings. A pivot on the lead foot turns the body a quarter turn in a fraction of a second and fans the long coat out flat; a full-weight overhead chop that misses drags the swinger past his target. A straight fist into the point of the chin snaps the head straight back and clacks the teeth; the fist is back at guard while the man still staggers. A heel stamped into the side of a loaded knee folds the joint inward and the man pitches forward onto his front. A palm that slaps a thrusting forearm down and inward pins the arm across the attacker's own body and leaves his face open in the same beat; a straight fist into the nose flattens it and snaps the head back. A hard yank on the wrist down past the hip drags the man forward and down into the elbow coming the other way; an elbow across the jaw whips the head sideways and springs the fingers open. A fist dropped like a hammer on the back of a bowed neck puts the man flat on his front. Ducking straight down under a flat swing leaves the swinger's arm wide and his face open. Four vertical fists down one rail in little over a second drive the head back four times and walk a big man backward. A side kick with the heel into the sternum, hip and shoulder stacked behind it, drives a heavy man one step back off his feet and onto his back. The coat whips on every move; between actions Adam is completely still in his narrow side-on stance.




LIGHTING — LOCKED TO THE LOCATION

The light in every shot is the light of @loc_club_dancefloor_v1 exactly as it is in the reference image — the same sources in the same places, with the same colour, direction and brightness, and the same darkness between them — copied as it is. Faces and bodies receive only what that light gives them where they stand; a face standing in the dark stays dark. The only changes to it are the locked strobe frame every 3 seconds, the fire cannon every 5 seconds described in SHOW RHYTHM, and, up close, the blue-white flicker of a baton during the swing it is switched on for.




AUDIO

Sound effects only, diegetic — bodies, voices, glass, fire, strobe, batons; the DJ is seen, not heard; no spoken words. The roar of a packed dancing hall, shouts, whistles and laughter the whole time, hundreds of feet on wet concrete; three sets of steps shoving through bodies; a switch click and a whip-crack of crackle born in a swing, three times in the block; a shaft whistling past an ear, a shaft whistling over a head; the leather coat snapping round on the pivot; the crack of knuckles on a chin and teeth clacking; the crunch of a heel into a knee; a body hitting wet concrete face-first; a palm slapping a forearm and the crack of knuckles on a nose in the same instant; the crack of an elbow on a jaw; the dull thud of a hammer fist on a neck; a second body hitting the floor; four fast cracks of knuckles on a face; the deep thump of a heel on a sternum and the air leaving a big man; a heavy back slamming onto wet concrete and water splashing; three batons clattering dead, one bouncing on its rings; Adam's steady breathing through the nose; the rasp of rings through hair; leather settling on shoulders; the party noise never dropping, swallowing all of it. The whoosh of a fire cannon every 5 seconds and the dry tick of the strobe every 3 seconds. No narration, no subtitles.




STYLE

Photoreal, contemporary Hollywood action texture, anamorphic cinema look, one long descent from above into a face and then a fight seen as a few razor-sharp frozen frames inside a dark moving crowd, the location's own colour exactly as in the reference, fine film grain, deep contrast with protected shadow detail.




QUALITY

Strikes whip-fast, each a short blur resolving into a crisp contact frame, the fist back at guard before the man has finished moving; each baton a single correctly shaped object, sparking only during its own swing and dark at every other moment; all ten strobe frames razor-sharp and on the clock, each one a single frame, none lasting longer, none on the first frame of a shot; the crane move in shot 1 smooth and continuous from straight down to the face with nothing but the crowd in frame; Adam's face sharp where it is the subject and his eyes unchanged from the reference; the three guards' faces unchanged from their references and never confused with each other; skin clean on every face, no blood anywhere; no merged bodies or duplicated limbs; stable cadence across twenty-one shots. Hard cuts, no dissolves.




POSITIVE CONSTRAINTS

Named characters: @char_adam_video_v1, @char_club_guard_1_image_v1, @char_club_guard_2_image_v1 and @char_club_guard_3_image_v1 — four men, no other guards, three different guard faces. Everything happens inside a packed dancing crowd with bodies overlapping everyone and no empty floor around anyone. Shot 1 is one continuous crane move from a top shot straight above Adam down and round to a close-up of his face, with three black suits converging on him from three edges of the frame. The three guards reach him at once from screen-left, screen-right and behind, each with a baton that is dark until his own swing; Adam takes them one at a time in this order: @char_club_guard_3_image_v1 from behind — pivot, straight fist to the chin, low sideways kick to the knee, face-down; @char_club_guard_2_image_v1 from screen-right — palm pins the thrust and a straight fist lands in the nose in the same beat, wrist yank into an elbow across the jaw, hammer fist to the back of the neck, face-down; @char_club_guard_1_image_v1 from screen-left — duck under the swing, four straight fists down the centre of the face, heel side kick into the sternum, onto his back. Every baton leaves its owner's hand dark and Adam never touches one. All three stay down. Adam goes completely still, pushes his hair back once with his right hand, settles his coat, and walks out of the right edge of the frame into the dancers; the block ends on the three men on the floor inside the dancing crowd with Adam gone. The crowd is ordinary club-goers in all kinds of different clothes, dancing packed tight the whole time, never standing still, never forming a ring or an audience around anyone, never scattering and not caring; it is visible in every shot and is never touched. Every character is framed at a three-quarter angle; every shot after the crane is handheld; the fight shots are under one and a half seconds each; real time, no slow motion. The strobe fires every 3 seconds on the clock, ten times, locked, never on the first frame of a shot; the fire cannon every 5 seconds, locked; the only other light is the location's own light and the sparks of a baton during its swing. Clean skin on every face and body — impacts show sweat and spit only. Frame 2.39:1 anamorphic.




FIGHT STYLE LOCK: every kick stays below belt height except the one heel into the sternum at waist height; every punch travels a short straight line launched from where the hands already rest at guard — the first visible motion of a limb IS the strike itself; between exchanges the fighter is completely still in his narrow side-on stance; the fight is short straight lines and long stillness.

Scene 3 — Corridor References

Build the narrow gallery and distinct guard lineup before choreographing the fight.

What it does: Establishes the narrow gallery corridor and its layout for the guards’ conversation and fight.

Cinematic wide interior shot, static camera at chest height, tucked close

to the RIGHT wall of a long corridor and rotated about 30 degrees toward

the left wall — a three-quarter view, deliberately asymmetric: the

corridor runs diagonally from bottom-right toward upper-left, the far end

in the left third of the frame, never centered, converging lines rushing

toward the single door at the end. Shot wide open at T2.0, shallow

anamorphic depth of field with a HEAVY defocused foreground: the focal

plane is a narrow slice resting on the first painting of the left wall

and the lit brass sconce beside it, about 4 meters in — everything nearer

and farther melts fast into deep anamorphic blur.




A dim residential corridor in an aging luxury hotel-apartment building,

15 meters long, 1.8 meters wide, ceiling 2.6 meters, low and heavy, with

NO doors along its walls — the only door is at the far end. Walls above

waist height in dull dark bottle-green wallpaper with a faint tonal

damask, faded unevenly, scuffed paler at hand height, one seam lifting;

below it a tall dado of dark walnut wainscoting with a dulled,

micro-scratched lacquer, heavy walnut rail and skirting. Ceiling painted

brown-black matte with a center row of recessed rectangular warm-white

fluorescent panels behind frosted diffusers every 3 meters, receding in

a shrinking rhythm, two dimmer and yellower, one faintly flickering.

Floor: wall-to-wall dark wool carpet with a small geometric pattern of

tiny squares and diamonds in chocolate-brown, umber and near-black,

crushed paler in a walking line down the middle, a faint stain near the

far end. On the LEFT wall: dark old oil paintings in tarnished gilt and

black-lacquered frames, unevenly spaced, slightly different sizes —

murky landscapes, a still life, a near-black abstract — cracked yellowed

varnish, gilt worn at the corners, one frame hanging crooked; no faces,

no readable text. Small brass sconces with cream half-shades between

them, most OFF, two lit low. At about 7 meters, a narrow walnut console

with a low-lit table lamp under a pleated cream shade and a tall dark

green ceramic floor vase with dry branches. On the RIGHT wall: bare

wallpaper for the first meters, then a long low bench in cracked dark

leather tight against the dado at about 6 meters, and two more dark

paintings beyond it. At the end, 15 meters away, ONE heavy dark walnut

door, flat-paneled, tall brass lever handle and kick plate mottled with

age, brass number UNREADABLE, set slightly off-center right, a thin line

of warm light beneath it. Nothing is perfectly straight — seams drift,

frames hang at different heights, a shade tilts, the bench sits a hand's

width off the wall. Worn, quiet, expensive once — never a clean render.




Foreground, heavily out of focus — surfaces only, nothing in the first

three meters: the near right wall is a thick defocused curtain the camera

shoots through, green wallpaper and walnut dado dissolving into a large

soft dark mass across the right third of the frame, an unlit sconce a

dim oval smear; the first ceiling panel overhead a wide soft warm-white

oval bloom at the top edge; the nearest carpet a soft dark wash along

the bottom.




Midground, in sharp focus: the first painting on the left at about

4 meters — tarnished gilt frame, cracked varnish over a dark landscape,

the lit sconce beside it glowing through its cream shade and pooling

warm light on the green damask and the carved frame edge, the walnut

rail beneath with a long soft sheen, the carpet pattern crisp at its

foot. The only truly sharp zone of the frame.




Background, deeply out of focus: the corridor recedes in oblique

foreshortening into layered dark-green planes, each milkier than the

last; the console lamp a large soft warm oval bloom, the vase a soft

vertical shape; further paintings soft dark rectangles with faint gilt

glints; the bench a low dark mass; the ceiling panels a shrinking

diagonal chain of large stretched warm-white oval highlights; the far

door a tall soft dark mass with a thin warm smear of light at its foot.

No readable shapes, no faces.




Focus falloff is progressive and optical: sharpness lives only on the

focal plane, blur increasing smoothly with distance in both directions,

frame edges and corners noticeably softer than the center, true

large-format shallow-focus depth.




Lighting: cinematic, soft and DARK — underexposed by about a stop and a

half, a true low-key night interior, but with LIFTED SHADOWS: the walls

between panels stay dim but readable, the paintings keep their murky

detail, the carpet keeps its pattern, nothing crushes to pure black, no

shadow pits. Primary: the recessed ceiling panels, warm-white around

3200K, glowing as soft rectangular halos, never visible rays, each

dropping a pool onto the carpet and grazing the frame tops and walnut

dado, pools separated by dim stretches — a receding rhythm of light and

shadow. Accent: two sconces and the console lamp at 2700K, warmer than

the panels, pooling amber on the green wallpaper and catching the gilt —

gentle warm-on-warm contrast. A thin warm line under the far door as the

deepest point of light. NO red, NO magenta, NO colored gels — warm white

and amber only. Specular life only where there is polish: small hard

glints on gilt edges, brass sconces and the far door handle, long soft

sheens down the walnut dado and the cracked leather; the carpet and

matte wallpaper give nothing back. Very faint, barely perceptible haze —

clean air at the camera, thickening gently with distance so the far

panels and the lamp bloom softly and the end sits in a faint milky

glow; no fog, no visible smoke. Heavy natural lens vignette, corners

dark but not black. Controlled highlights, no blown whites on the

diffusers or shades.




Color palette: dull bottle-green and green-black, dark walnut,

chocolate-brown and umber carpet, brown-black ceiling, tarnished gilt,

aged brass, cracked dark leather, warm-white and amber tungsten;

absolutely no red, burgundy or crimson anywhere; no saturated greens, no

neons, no uniform yellow filter, no teal-and-orange wash; blacks

slightly warm and milky; rich low-key contrast, deep velvety textured

shadows, soft rolled-off highlights, gently desaturated — a nocturnal,

romantic-melancholic grade of an old private gallery corridor, faintly

faded like an old print.




Mood: a long dark corridor of old paintings leading to a single closed

door — hushed, heavy, tender and lonely.




Empty location: no people, no characters, no human presence, no

silhouettes, no human shadows, no faces in the paintings. Only the

interior described — no other furniture, no carts, no boxes, no signs,

no readable text, no clutter.




Shot on a REAL 35mm film camera with vintage anamorphic lenses — the

image carries honest optical imperfections of physical glass, not a

digital look.




35mm anamorphic film still. STRONG anamorphic lens character, turned

up to maximum: heavy horizontal squeeze and compression, large

pronounced oval elliptical bokeh, boldly stretched highlights,

curved barrel edge distortion clearly bending straight lines near

the frame edges, visible chromatic aberration fringing toward the

borders.




HEAVY ANAMORPHIC FALLOFF: the focal plane is a narrow slice —

everything off it melts fast and deep. Strong optical softening

claims the outer thirds of the frame: toward the left and right

edges the image smears into thick oval-shaped anamorphic blur, the

corners heavily soft and darkened by natural optical vignetting;

foreground elements near the lens dissolve into large creamy

defocused masses sweeping across the frame; defocused highlights

bloom into big vertical-oval shapes. The falloff is smooth and

optical, never a digital blur overlay or a sharp mask — real

anamorphic glass wide open, melting everything it does not love.




Fine organic 35mm film grain, more visible in the shadows, soft

halation on the brightest highlights — the in-focus sconce, the nearest

ceiling panel, the console lamp, the light under the far door.

Real-world texture: rubbed wallpaper, cracked varnish and worn gilt,

fingerprints on brass, cracked leather, crushed carpet pile — no

plastic surfaces, no clean CGI geometry. NO lens flares, NO light

streaks, NO floating bokeh circles. 2.39:1.

Soul cinema extras corridor

What it does: Defines five distinct guards so each remains recognizable during the corridor sequence.

Photoreal full-body group reference of five men, one yakuza crew, a wall of muscle standing shoulder to shoulder in a straight line, evenly spaced, all facing the camera, wide heavy stance, weight forward, shoulders squared, hands hanging loose or clasped low, completely still. Every one of them is physically imposing: tall, 185 to 200 cm, heavy bone structure, thick necks, wide shoulders, big hands, the bodies of heavyweight fighters and enforcers — nobody thin, nobody frail, nobody slouching. Every face hard and unreadable: no smiles, jaws set, brows low, eyes locked on the camera with a cold, patient, threatening stare. Full body from the top of the head to the boots, nobody cropped, nobody overlapping. Single plain light gray background, seamless continuous gray background, clean studio light from the front with hard shadows under the brows and cheekbones, no shadows on the backdrop. No panel borders, no dividers, no frames, no numbers, no labels, no text. Camera at chest height, straight-on, 50mm lens, every man in sharp focus.

WARDROBE — one crew, one dress code, all black, near-identical: every man wears a dark black jacket (black leather jacket or black bomber jacket, matte, well-worn), a plain black shirt or black t-shirt underneath, black straight trousers, black heavy boots. No color anywhere on any of them — no white, no patterns, no prints, no bright accessories. Differences between the men are only in build, face, haircut, tattoos and small details: a collar open or closed, a jacket zipped or unzipped, one thin gold chain, one steel watch. Tattoos are traditional Japanese irezumi, dark and muted, showing on necks, chests and hands only where the black clothes leave skin open.

Left to right — East Asian men, 30 to 50, all heavy and dangerous, each different in face and haircut:

1 — black leather jacket zipped to the chest, black shirt. Long face, sharp cheekbones, short black hair combed back tight, clean-shaven. Irezumi visible on the back of both hands.

2 — black bomber jacket open over a black shirt unbuttoned to the sternum, dense irezumi across the chest, one thin gold chain. Round heavy face, heavy brow, short cropped hair.

3 — the TALLEST. Black bomber jacket open over a black t-shirt, irezumi on the neck and the backs of the hands. Holds a MACHETE in his right hand, blade pointing straight down along the leg, dull dark steel, black grip, held like a tool. Hollow cheeks, buzz cut, heavy-lidded eyes.

4 — the WIDEST, a head taller than most, massive shoulders and torso. Black leather jacket closed, black shirt, black tie. Wide face, thick neck, shaved head, small deep-set eyes.

5 — boxer's build, thick arms. Black bomber jacket open over a black turtleneck, irezumi climbing the side of the neck, knuckles scarred and heavy. Square face, flattened boxer's nose, short black crop.

Cinematic still photography, natural skin texture, no retouching, no stylization. 16:9.

重新创作
Scene 3.1 — The Guards’ Conversation

What it does: Introduces the guards through a casual argument about vampire films, then shifts their attention to Adam.

SCENE CONTEXT

30 seconds, ONE CONTINUOUS HANDHELD TAKE, shot from the hands of a man who cannot hold a camera still — the frame shakes violently in every single frame, from the first to the last. Hotel corridor, @loc_corridor_v1 — the long gallery corridor hung with framed paintings. Five guards stand in the middle of the corridor facing each other in a loose ring, blocking it, killing a shift with a short argument in Japanese about vampire films in general — the old ones against the new ones — the frame jittering, lurching and tilting around them even now. At the far end Adam walks in, hands empty. One guard sees him; the camera whips down the corridor to Adam in a smear — and whips straight back to the ring, where all five faces have already turned and are going hard, and they break and charge as one wave. Adam charges too. He runs at them and leaves the ground: a flying kick that drives the first man off his feet. The second arrives and takes an elbow across the jaw and goes down. The third throws a punch; Adam's forearm knocks it off its line and three short strikes go into the body and a fourth into the face, and he folds. The fourth, the biggest, roars and runs — the take ramps into slow motion and Adam's lead fist is already in his jaw, and he drops. The fifth swings a big right; Adam ducks under it and drives a fist straight up into his groin — the man grabs himself and folds double — and Adam's knee comes up into his face: the man's head snaps back, his whole body straightens up off the knee, and he goes over backward and lands flat on his back, out. Real speed. Adam goes still — and the operator drops to the carpet among the bodies, so the camera shoots him from the ground, from below, shaking, as he shakes out his coat, squares it on his shoulders and breathes out. He walks on over the bodies and the camera scrambles after him along the floor — and only now his right hand goes inside his coat and comes out with a Desert Eagle, which swings down into the low frame and hangs at his thigh as he walks. Cut. Shot as if by one operator holding the camera in his bare hands with no rig, no support and no rest — running when they run, whipping so hard the image smears, thrown by every impact, clipping the wall, ending up on the floor. Neo-noir with a dry comic turn: the fight is short straight lines and long stillness, and the camera is the one thing in the corridor that never, for a single frame, stops shaking.




ACTIVE REFERENCES

@char_adam_video_v1 — VIDEO ELEMENT — ADAM. Face, build, coat, boots, chain 100% match. Face shown throughout, in three-quarter or profile, and from below at the end. Reference controls identity. He fights like a man trained in one thing: a narrow side-on stance, right shoulder and right foot leading, and short straight lines — with one leap and one knee in this fight, and everything else straight. His hands are empty throughout; the pistol stays inside his coat until the last five seconds.

@char_corridor_guards_image_v1 — THE GUARDS. A line-up reference: five men from it — the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 5th and 6th from the left — are the five guards in this corridor, each 100% his man in face, body and clothes. GUARD 1 (1st on the sheet) — the sweater, the last one, the groin and the knee. GUARD 2 (2nd) — the hothead, the one who takes the flying kick. GUARD 3 (4th) — the professional, the one who takes the body shots. GUARD 4 (5th) — the showman, the one who takes the elbow. GUARD 5 (6th) — the bull, the slow-motion one. They are unarmed; their hands are empty from first frame to last. Five men, each appearing once, their skin and clothes clean throughout.

@prop_adam_pistol — ADAM'S PISTOL, a Desert Eagle in .50 Action Express, 100% as the reference — a huge slab of a pistol, nearly two kilos of steel, six-inch barrel, the weight of it visible in the wrist. In this scene it is never fired: it lives inside the left breast of Adam's coat, unseen, until 26s, when his right hand draws it and it hangs muzzle-down at his thigh as he walks out of the scene. The one pistol in the scene.

@loc_corridor_v1 — THE CORRIDOR. Set, materials, light, colour, atmosphere 100% as the reference, including the framed paintings in a row along the sconce wall. About 30 m long here.




LOCATION MAP

A long straight corridor, about 30 m long, 2.5 m wide, 3 m high, carpeted, sconces and framed paintings along one wall at eye level, one painting roughly every 4 m; the opposite wall plain. THE RING: 6 m in from the near end the five stand facing each other across the corridor in a loose ring that blocks it — GUARD 4 and GUARD 2 with their backs toward the painting wall, facing across; GUARD 1 and GUARD 5 opposite them with their backs toward the plain wall; GUARD 3 at the far side of the ring, half-turned so one shoulder faces the group and his eyes have the long empty corridor beyond them. ADAM'S ENTRY: the far end, 20 m from the ring; he walks, then runs, toward the ring with the painting wall on his right and the plain wall on his left. THE COLLISION: about 10 m from the ring, where the two runs meet; the fight happens across the width of the corridor there. THE OPERATOR: starts at the near end, 3 m behind the ring, camera in his bare hands at chest height, runs behind the guards, slides along the plain wall on Adam's left for the fight, drops to the carpet among the bodies at 20.5s and stays down there, crawling after Adam, to the cut.




FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING

Handheld, camera in two bare hands at chest height, already shaking hard — a fast jitter and a slow lurch on top of it, in the very first frame — 3 m behind the ring at the near end, angled at three-quarter so the ring sits diagonally: GUARD 4's back and shoulder nearest the lens, half-turned toward the others mid-sentence, GUARD 2 across from him with a hand up, GUARD 1 beside GUARD 2 with his arms folded, GUARD 5 huge at the far side of the ring with his arms hanging, GUARD 3 beyond them half-turned, his face toward the empty corridor. Past the ring the corridor runs away 20 m to the far end, empty. The horizon is already ten degrees off and swinging. Exactly five people in the first frame; six in the scene.




FORMAT MODE

One continuous handheld take, 30 seconds, one camera in one man's bare hands, one lens, one speed ramp into slow motion at 14.5–16.5s and back. The shake is the format, and it is heavy: at no point in the thirty seconds does the frame hold for even a quarter of a second. Two layers of it at all times — a fast fine jitter from the hands, and on top of it a slow heavy lurch from the body — and in the action a third: violent whips that smear the image into streaks, running strides that slam the frame up and down, impacts that throw the whole picture sideways and let it claw back. The horizon swings ten to twenty degrees and is never level. In the slow-motion passage the shake slows with the picture into a wide, heaving, tilted drift that never comes to rest; when the speed snaps back so does the full shake. At the end the camera goes down to the carpet and shakes there — the operator's breathing and his hands on the floor making the low frame tremble and rock — and crawls after Adam shaking. There is no still frame in this take.




OPTICS

One lens for the whole take: 50 mm on full frame, about 47° diagonal — a tight lens for a corridor, which magnifies every tremor of the hands into a visible shudder of the whole picture; the operator gets his close-ups by stepping in and his wides by backing to the wall. Anamorphic character: oval bokeh on the sconces, horizontally stretched highlights, gentle barrel curve at the edges, shallow focus that the operator rides by hand and loses — a beat of soft on every fast refocus, focus breathing under the shake, sconces smeared into streaks on every whip.




CAMERA — one unbroken, violently shaking path

0–4.5s: handheld around the outside of the ring, three-quarter, the frame jittering fast and lurching slow the whole time, sliding half a metre in every direction as the operator shifts his feet, tipping toward whoever is speaking and overshooting him, tilting, faces at mid-shot and never centred for long. 4.5–5.5s: a hard whip-pan off GUARD 3's face down the length of the corridor to Adam — the picture smearing into streaks, overshooting him, jerking back past him, jerking back again, shaking — a beat of soft focus snapping sharp on him, small and walking. 5.5–6.5s: a hard whip-pan straight back to the ring, the picture smearing, landing on all five faces at once in one shaking mid-wide frame as they turn and go ugly together. 6.5–8s: the men break past the lens and knock the camera round with them — a real hit, the picture spinning half a turn and finding them — and the operator runs, the frame slamming up and down with every stride, five backs bouncing ahead under the paintings, and beyond them Adam, running at the lens, closing fast. 8–9.5s: still running, the horizon rocking twenty degrees each way, the operator veering to the plain wall and clipping it — a hard jolt — as Adam leaves the ground and the kick lands in a smear of coat; the frame is thrown. 9.5–14s: the operator slides along the plain wall to Adam's left three-quarter, full bodies in frame, the camera shaking hard from the run and never calming, thrown on every impact — the elbow, the block, the three body shots, the face — and clawing back each time. 14–14.5s: a smearing whip to GUARD 5 as he comes. 14.5–16.5s: the take ramps into slow motion, the shake slowing into a wide heaving tilted drift, the lens at chin height swimming after the lead fist into the jaw. 16.5–19.5s: real speed, the full shake snapping back; the camera drops with Adam's duck, is thrown by the groin shot, dips with the folding man, and is jerked upward by the knee, following the man's head as it snaps back and his body goes over. 19.5–20.5s: the camera trembles and edges around Adam while he stands still. 20.5–23.5s: the operator drops to the carpet — the frame falling, hitting the floor, bouncing — and shoots Adam from the ground, the camera lying on the carpet among the bodies, 15 cm off the floor, tilted up at him, three-quarter from his left and below, his whole figure towering against the ceiling and the sconces, the low frame trembling and rocking with the operator's breathing and his hands on the floor. 23.5–30s: the camera stays on the ground and crawls after Adam as he walks — 20–30 cm off the carpet, bouncing and shaking with the operator's scramble, the bodies passing in the foreground, Adam's boots and coat hem and the backs of his legs ahead, tilted up enough to hold his back and shoulders, dipping as the pistol swings down into frame at his thigh. Every angle a three-quarter or a profile, and no angle holds for a quarter of a second.




ACTION TIMING — stages of one shaking take




0–4.5s — Five men in a ring, killing a shift, the frame jittering and lurching around them. The argument is vampire films in general — the old ones against the new — and these lines are the whole of it. GUARD 4, back to the painting wall, one hand carving the air at the man across from him: 「吸血鬼の映画、昔のが一番だろ。」 GUARD 2, facing him, takes a half-step in with a hand up: 「いや、最近のほうが怖いって。」 GUARD 1, beside him, arms folded, admits it to the carpet: 「…俺は全部怖かった。」 GUARD 4 laughs at him across the ring. GUARD 5 grunts, unimpressed by all of them, and rolls a shoulder. GUARD 3, at the far side, is not in it: his head is turned, his eyes on the long empty corridor. The camera shudders, tips from speaker to speaker, overshoots each one, never lands square on any of them.




4.5–5.5s — GUARD 3's head stops. Low, flat, to the others: 「おい。」 The camera whips off his face and down the corridor in a smear, overshoots, jerks back: at the far end, small, Adam has walked in and is walking toward them, hands empty at his sides, coat closed, eyes on the ring, the frame shaking around his small figure.




5.5–6.5s — The camera whips straight back to the ring and lands on all five at once, shaking: five heads already turned down the corridor, and in one second the argument is gone from every face and something else is in them — GUARD 2's head pushing forward off his neck and his teeth showing, GUARD 4's grin gone hard, GUARD 1's jaw clenching, GUARD 5's growl coming up out of his chest as his shoulders roll, GUARD 3 already sinking a centimetre into his legs. One beat of five men going ugly together, and they go.




6.5–8s — They break past the lens as a wave, and one of them knocks the camera: the picture spins half a turn, finds the corridor again, shaking. GUARD 2 out in front, a shout tearing out of him; GUARD 4 a step behind him yelling; GUARD 3 low and straight and silent; GUARD 5 heavy and fast a beat later, roaring; GUARD 1 last, running shouting to keep himself running. The operator runs: five backs strung out down the corridor under the paintings, the frame slamming with every stride — and beyond them Adam has broken into a run too, straight at them, coat flying open behind him, closing the twenty metres from both ends.




8–9.5s — Ten metres from the ring the two runs meet, and Adam leaves the ground. Two strides out he launches — the lead knee folding up to his chest in the air and the leg pistoning out sideways, heel leading, hip and shoulder stacked in one straight brace from shoulder to heel — and the flat of his boot slams into GUARD 2's chest and jaw like stamping a heavy door open. GUARD 2 leaves his feet and flies straight back two metres, folding around the boot, and lands flat on his back and does not get up. Adam comes down out of the kick on both feet in the narrow side-on stance, hands already at guard. The camera is thrown by the kick, clips the plain wall with a jolt and comes back shaking.




9.5–11s — GUARD 4 arrives a step behind the first man, arms wide, coming to grab, and gets inside Adam's reach — which is where Adam wants him: chest to chest, Adam's right arm folds and the point of the elbow slices a short flat arc across GUARD 4's jaw, the whole torso turning a few degrees behind it. The head snaps sideways, the grin goes, the legs go, and GUARD 4 drops straight down onto the carpet on his side and does not move. The frame is thrown by the elbow.




11–14s — GUARD 3 arrives low and straight and throws a hard right at Adam's head. Adam's left forearm comes up inside the swing and knocks it a hand's width off its line — the fist glancing past his ear — and he is inside the man's arm. Three short strikes into the body, each one a state already landing, the camera shocked harder on each: a short scooping hook digs up under the floating ribs from a palm's width away, knuckles half-turned up, and the man folds sideways around the spot; his rear fist drives straight through into the solar plexus, hips snapping over, and the man's breath leaves him in a bark and his chin drops; his lead arm bends and hooks tight into the ribs on the other side, elbow staying down, torso turning only a few degrees, and the man's knees dip and his hands come off his guard. Then the fourth: his lead fist fires from exactly where it hovers — one straight line to the chin, knuckles vertical, thumb up, elbow unfolding behind the fist, a short glide-step feeding body weight into it. The head snaps back hard, the man's hands leave his body, and he drops straight down onto the carpet on his back and lies still, the frame lurching down after him and back up to Adam. Adam's hand is already back at guard.




14–16.5s — GUARD 5, the bull, has seen it — three men down in six seconds — and it puts fire in him: he roars and comes at Adam flat out, head down, fists up, huge. The operator lunges in, the frame heaving, and at 14.5s the take ramps into slow motion — the shake slowing with it into a wide, heaving, tilted drift — the lens at chin height from Adam's left at three-quarter: the bull is still a stride out, his right shoulder loading — and in that exact instant Adam's lead fist is already landing on the point of his jaw, the straight line beating the curve, launched from exactly where the hand rested at guard, knuckles vertical, thumb up, elbow unfolding behind the fist, a long glide-step driving his whole body weight down the line of the arm into the running man. The slow motion holds on the contact while the frame swims: the jaw deforms around the knuckles, the lower face pushed across, the eyes rolling up, the head snapping back and up so hard the neck arches and the big shoulders follow it, the run dying under him, his own forward weight doubling the hit. His skin stays clean. Adam's face over his own fist: flat, eyes on the point of contact. At 16.5s the take ramps back to real speed and the full shake snaps back: the bull's knees fold and he goes down rigid, straight back, flat on his back on the carpet with a floor-shaking thud that throws the whole frame, arms out, and does not move.




16.5–19.5s — GUARD 1, the last, arrives shouting and swings a huge wide right at Adam's head — everything he has, eyes half shut for it. Adam's knees fold and his whole body drops a head's height straight down under the arm — the fist passes through the air where his skull was, the camera dropping with him — and from down there, in the same beat, his right fist fires straight up from the guard into GUARD 1's groin, one short vertical line, the knuckles landing with a flat wet thump. Every muscle in GUARD 1 locks at once. A high, breathless sound comes out of him — not a scream, the air leaving — and both his hands clamp between his legs and he folds double, forward, over the pain, his face coming down to the height of Adam's waist. Adam is already rising: both his hands clamp the back of GUARD 1's neck and drag the head down as his right knee drives up — and the knee meets the face with everything Adam's rising body has in it. The head snaps back and up off the knee, the whole folded body straightens with it — upright for one frame, arms flying out from between his legs, eyes already gone — and he goes over backward, stiff, and lands flat on his back on the carpet with a thud that shakes the floor, arms out, out cold. The camera is jerked upward with the knee and follows him over and down, shaking.




19.5–20.5s — Adam settles back into the narrow side-on stance and goes completely still — feet planted, hands frozen at guard, only his eyes tracking over the lead shoulder across the five men on the floor. The stillness is his alone: the camera trembles, breathes and edges sideways around him for the full second, the frame never resting on him.




20.5–23.5s — The operator goes down. The frame drops, hits the carpet, bounces, and comes to lie among the bodies, 15 cm off the floor, tilted up — and there is Adam from the ground: a black figure towering over the lens against the ceiling and the sconces, three-quarter from his left and below, the fallen men's shoulders and boots in the foreground of the low frame, the whole picture trembling and rocking with the operator's breathing on the floor. His hands come down. He takes the coat by both lapels and shakes it out — one hard shake, the black cloth snapping and falling straight — and squares it on his shoulders with one tug and settles the collar, seen from below, huge. One long breath out through the mouth, the shoulders dropping with it, the breath visible in the chest from down here.




23.5–30s — He walks — the same pace he came in with — on down the corridor over the bodies toward where the ring stood, and the camera stays on the ground and scrambles after him: 20–30 cm off the carpet, bouncing hard with the operator's crawl, the fallen men sliding past in the foreground — the sweater flat on his back, the bull's outflung arm, the professional flat, the showman on his side, the hothead flat on his back — Adam's boots and the swinging hem of the coat and the backs of his legs ahead, the frame tilted up enough to hold his back and shoulders, everything shaking. And as he walks, at 26s, his right hand goes up inside the left breast of his coat — above the low frame — and comes back down with the Desert Eagle, the big pistol swinging down into the picture and hanging at his thigh, muzzle down, moving a little with his stride, huge in the low frame, the camera jolting after it along the floor, the paintings and sconces juddering past high above. Hard cut at 30 on his back and the pistol at his side mid-stride, the frame still shaking on the carpet.




SCENE DIRECTION (shared, unspoken — all six play it): make it look like nothing. Five men stand in a ring in the middle of a corridor arguing about films because a man at the end of it is nothing; when he turns out to be something they go at him five on one because that is still nothing. Adam plays the same line from his end — he runs at five men because waiting would mean they were something, the fight is a chore, the coat gets shaken out afterward because nothing happened, and the gun comes out only when the corridor is his, for whoever is behind the door. The event is the moment "nothing" turns out to be a very expensive word, and it ends with the one man who never dropped the line walking away down the corridor with the thing he came to use — seen from the floor, where everyone else is.




ACTING TASK — ADAM (fully invested; the work is in his eyes and his stillness):

MOTIVE (his fuel): a man to see at the end of this corridor and a clock running.

GOAL: get past five men spending as little time as possible — and keep the gun for the one who deserves it.

OBSTACLE: five men who want him to stop walking, and a body that wants to enjoy this — the fighter in him wants the bull, and letting him have it costs time.

TACTIC: when they run he runs, because the fastest way through five men is into them, and the first one is close enough to take out of the air. After that each man gets exactly what he brings: the one who comes inside gets the elbow, the one who punches gets a forearm and the body opened up, the one who runs gets a straight line, and the one who swings for the sky gets the floor under him taken away — the duck, the groin, the knee — because a man who swings that big has already told you where he will be. The gun never enters it: these men are not what the gun is for. Between each he is still and his eyes are already on the next man. He never plays the fight to them; he plays the clock. Afterward the coat, the breath and the draw are the only things he allows himself.

Moment to moment:

— the entrance — he walks and lets five men look at him; his eyes count them and settle on the one who saw him first

— the run — the moment they break, he breaks; eyes on the first man, measuring the range for the leap

— the leap — eyes on the first chest through the air; he lands already looking at the second

— the elbow — the man is inside; the elbow is the shortest thing he has; the eyes are already past him

— the block — he watches the shoulder, not the fist; the forearm and the first body shot are one decision

— the body, the chin — each strike is a fact, not an effort; the face does not change; the eyes stay on the man's centre until the chin

— the bull — he watches the shoulder begin to load and is already moving down the line; the eyes are on the jaw

— the big swing — he watches it wind up and simply is not there; the eyes go to the groin as the fist goes there

— the fold — hands on the neck, knee up; the eyes on the face coming down to meet it

— the fall — he watches the man go over backward the way a man watches a door close

— the stillness — a full second; the eyes travel over the five, checking that each one is done

— the coat — shaken out the way a man shakes rain off it; the eyes on the far end of the corridor already

— the breath, the draw — a man tidying up and taking out what he came with; the eyes never come down to the gun

(Safety: eyes engaged on real targets throughout — the men, the shoulder, the jaw, the groin, the face, the corridor ahead — never glassy; natural blink cadence; the calm is attention, not absence.)




ACTING TASK — GUARD 1, the sweater (the work is in the eyes and the folded arms):

MOTIVE: the others must not see that he is afraid — of the film, of the man, of anything.

GOAL: be one of them.

OBSTACLE: he is the one who admits all the films scared him, and he is last off the mark, and by the time he arrives four men are on the floor.

TACTIC: arms folded so the hands have nothing to do; a laugh half a beat late; he goes when they go and runs shouting so the shouting will carry him. He arrives last to a corridor full of his own men lying down, and the only thing left to do is swing as big as he can, because a small swing would mean he was afraid — so he shuts his eyes and gives it everything, and the everything goes through empty air. What comes up from below is the last thing on earth he was watching for.

— 「…俺は全部怖かった。」 — to the carpet, then a quick look up: did they laugh? they did; he laughs too

— the turn — his face goes hard because the faces around him do

— the run — last to go, eyes on the backs ahead — and then on the backs on the floor

— the swing — everything he has, wide, eyes half shut for the last of it

— the groin — the eyes fly open and see nothing; the sound is the air leaving; the hands go where the pain is

— the knee — the eyes on the carpet, then the ceiling, then nothing




ACTING TASK — GUARD 2, the hothead (the work is in the eyes and the feet):

MOTIVE: he is right about films and he is right about this corridor, and both need proving.

GOAL: be first to the man.

OBSTACLE: four men who make it a group decision.

TACTIC: he argues the way he fights — the half-step in, the hand up. When the camera comes back to the ring his face is already the first one gone hard, and he is running before the others have finished deciding. He is first, as he wanted, and the last thing he sees is a boot coming at him through the air.

— 「いや、最近のほうが怖いって。」 — the half-step into GUARD 4, eyes hunting for the back-down

— the turn — the first face to change; teeth, the head pushing forward off the neck

— the run — first to go, shouting, eyes on Adam's eyes; the eyes going wide as Adam leaves the ground




ACTING TASK — GUARD 3, the professional (the work is entirely in the eyes):

MOTIVE: he has done this before, and the ones who went down were talking.

GOAL: see it coming.

OBSTACLE: four men making noise in a ring around him, and a man who does what he has never seen done.

TACTIC: he stands at the edge of the ring half-turned and watches the corridor while the others watch each other; he says the one word. He goes low and straight, lets the loud ones arrive first and watches two of them go down in three seconds, and throws his punch clean and hard at the face — the right move against any man he has met before — and finds the man inside his arm.

— the talk — not in it; his eyes on the empty corridor, checking it the way a man checks a lock

— 「おい。」 — flat, to the others, eyes staying on Adam; a report, not an alarm

— the turn — he sinks into his legs while the others make faces

— the run — low, straight, silent; a half-step slower than the loud ones on purpose

— the punch — thrown at the face because that is where the man's eyes are; the forearm is already there




ACTING TASK — GUARD 4, the showman (the work is in the eyes — his crew's eyes as much as Adam's):

MOTIVE: the story of tonight, told later, must have him in it.

GOAL: be looked at.

OBSTACLE: nobody is looking at him for long.

TACTIC: he plays every line across the ring and checks after each whether it landed; when the camera comes back his grin goes hard because the others' faces have, and he runs on the hothead's heels because that is where the picture will be. He arrives a step behind the first man, arms wide for the grab that people see — and gets inside the reach of a man who wanted him there.

— 「吸血鬼の映画、昔のが一番だろ。」 — across the ring, hand carving the air, eyes going round for agreement

— the laugh at GUARD 1 — head back, then a check: are the others laughing with him

— the turn — the grin hardening late, a flick of the eyes round the ring to match the others

— the run — a step behind the hothead, yelling, arms coming wide

— the elbow — the eyes on Adam's chest a foot away, then on nothing




ACTING TASK — GUARD 5, the bull (the work is in the eyes and the weight):

MOTIVE: he is the one who finishes things; the others are the opening act.

GOAL: be the man who reaches Adam.

OBSTACLE: his own weight — slow to start, impossible to stop — and three men in his way who are down before he gets there.

TACTIC: he takes no part in the film talk; he grunts at it and rolls a shoulder. He does not look at Adam's face; he looks at the middle of the chest, where he is going to put his fist. He goes a beat late and watches three men drop in front of him, and every one of them is fuel: this is his now. He runs flat out, head down, the right shoulder already loading a stride out — and never sees the straight line coming.

— the talk — a grunt; a shoulder rolled; eyes on nothing

— the turn — the growl comes up out of the chest; the eyes fix on Adam's chest and stay there

— the run — a beat late; eyes on the chest through the three men going down; the roar

— the lights go out mid-stride




PHYSICS

The camera is two bare hands and a running, falling body: a fast jitter and a slow lurch in every frame; whips that smear the picture; running strides that slam it; impacts that throw it sideways and let it claw back; a wall clipped with a jolt; a knock from a passing man that spins it; a fall to the carpet that bounces it; and on the floor the trembling and rocking of a man breathing and crawling with a camera in his hands. The horizon swings ten to twenty degrees and is never level. In the slow-motion ramp the shake slows into a wide heaving drift and snaps back with real speed. Five men in a ring argue at five rhythms, turn hard together in one second and break as one wave at five speeds; Adam breaks with them and two runs close twenty metres from both ends. The flying kick: two strides and a launch, the lead knee folding to the chest in the air and the leg pistoning out sideways, heel leading, shoulder and hip stacked in one straight brace behind it — the flat of the boot meets chest and jaw with the whole flying mass behind it, the man leaves his feet, flies two metres straight back folding around the boot and lands flat on his back; Adam comes down on both feet into his stance. The elbow: chest to chest, the arm folds and the point of the elbow travels a short flat arc across the jaw with a few degrees of torso behind it — the head snaps sideways, the legs go, the man drops on his side. The block is a forearm inside the swing knocking the fist a hand's width off its line past the ear; the body shots fold the man around each point of contact — sideways for the hook under the ribs, forward for the straight into the solar plexus, a dip of the knees for the hook to the far ribs — and the straight to the chin snaps the head back and drops him straight down on his back. The slow-motion straight lead lands as the bull's shoulder is still loading a stride out: the straight line beats the curve, the jaw deforms around the knuckles, the head snaps back and up, the run dies under him, his own forward weight doubles the impact, and he falls rigid onto his back with a floor-shaking thud, unconscious. The last man: a wide swing passes through the air where a head was, because the head has dropped a full head's height straight down on folded knees; from the crouch a fist travels one short vertical line up into the groin and lands with a flat thump; every muscle in the struck man locks, the air leaves him in a high breathless sound, both hands clamp between his legs and he folds double, face coming down to waist height; two hands clamp the back of his neck and drag the head down as a knee drives up with the whole rising body behind it — the knee meets the face, the head snaps back and up, the folded body is straightened by the blow, the arms fly out, and he goes over backward stiff and lands flat on his back with a floor-shaking thud, unconscious. The coat, shaken out by both lapels, snaps and falls straight with the weight of heavy black cloth. Skin stays clean on every man; clothes stay whole. The Desert Eagle, drawn at the end, is nearly two kilos: the wrist shows the weight as it comes down out of the coat and it hangs heavy at the thigh, swinging a little with the stride. Adam steps over bodies with the weight of a walking man; the camera crawls after him along the floor.




LIGHTING

Practical only, exactly as @loc_corridor_v1: the sconce wall throws warm hard side-light across the paintings, the ring of men and the carpet, the plain wall falls to shadow, the far end darker than the near; under the shake the sconces smear into short streaks on every whip and jitter as points the rest of the time, and Adam's leap crosses one sconce pool and one shadow. When the lens is close on a face, the near side is lit hard and the far side falls to shadow. In the slow-motion passage the sconce light rakes across the bull's jaw and Adam's knuckles at the moment of contact. From the floor at the end, Adam is a dark figure against the lit ceiling and the row of sconces above him, the side-light catching his jaw and the edge of the coat as it snaps; the drawn pistol picks up a sconce along its slide as it swings down into the low frame.




AUDIO

Ambience: faint bass through the walls from the club, sconce hum, carpet underfoot, five men shifting their weight in a ring, the operator's breathing close to the mic and the constant creak, rustle and knock of the camera in his bare hands throughout — and at the end, the scrape of the camera and his elbows on the carpet.

[Dialogue — spoken in Japanese, natural native delivery, casual, overlapping edges]

0.5–1.7s — GUARD 4: 「吸血鬼の映画、昔のが一番だろ。」

1.7–2.7s — GUARD 2: 「いや、最近のほうが怖いって。」

2.8–4.0s — GUARD 1: 「…俺は全部怖かった。」 — GUARD 4's short laugh over the end of it; GUARD 5's grunt.

4.7–5.1s — GUARD 3, flat, low: 「おい。」

Nothing else is spoken. GUARD 1 makes one high breathless sound when the groin shot lands — air, not words. Adam says nothing in the scene.

[SFX] Adam's boots on carpet from the far end, walking, then breaking into a run. The turn — five breaths going hard, a growl rising. The break — boots, one shout, then a yell, then a roar, then one more voice, ragged; a body knocking the camera with a thump; the operator's running breath and footfalls under everything from 6.5s to 9.5s; boots hammering carpet at different rhythms from both directions, converging. Two hard steps, a whip of coat in the air, and one flat heavy thump of a boot sole on a chest and jaw, a body flying and landing flat on carpet two metres away; Adam's boots hitting the floor; the camera knocking against the wall. The hard sharp crack of an elbow on a jaw and a body dropping onto its side. The whip of GUARD 3's punch and the slap of a forearm on a forearm; three short body impacts — a deep thud under the ribs, a bark of breath driven out, a thud to the far ribs — and one clean crack of knuckles on a chin, a body dropping flat. The bull's roar and heavy running boots. The ramp: the sound stretches and drops — the roar slowed into a low groan, the air moving ahead of the fist — one deep, dense crack of knuckles on jawbone with a long ring after it — and the sound snaps back to speed as a very heavy body hits the carpet flat on its back, the floor shaking. A shout and the whip of a huge swing through empty air; a flat wet thump low down, and a high thin breathless sound, a whole body locking; two hands slapping onto the back of a neck; the deep crunching thud of a knee meeting a face, and a stiff body going over and slamming flat onto its back. The operator dropping to the floor — knees, elbows, the camera thumping the carpet. Silence with a ring in it, one full second, the operator's breath heaving against the floor. Fabric: heavy black cloth snapped once, hard, and two hands squaring a coat on the shoulders. One long exhale, close, above the mic. Boots on carpet, steady, over and around bodies, walking away; the operator's elbows and knees scraping and thumping after them along the floor, his breath ragged. The coat opening above, and the heavy slide of the Desert Eagle coming down out of it, the weight of it settling at the end of the arm. Boots, the crawl, to the cut. No music.




STYLE

Neo-noir with a dry comic turn, one continuous take from a man's bare hands on a 50 mm: heavy grain, warm sconce light across a gallery of dark paintings, deep shadow, anamorphic ovals and stretched highlights smeared by the shake, the jittering frame, the lurching frame, the running frame, the thrown frame, the frame that falls to the floor and shakes there — never once, in thirty seconds, a frame that holds; the film argument played loose and real, the turn of five faces played in one shaking frame, a man running at five runners and taking the first out of the air; the fight is short straight lines and long stillness — one leap, one knee, and everything else a straight line from the guard — inside a picture that never stops moving, with one speed ramp into a single straight punch as the only ornament, the coat shaken out and the gun drawn seen from the carpet, from where the five are lying.




QUALITY

One unbroken take with no cut anywhere; a heavy, visible handheld shake in every single frame from first to last — jitter and lurch at the calmest, smearing whips and slamming strides and thrown frames in the action, a wide heaving drift in the slow motion, a trembling rocking low frame on the floor at the end; five distinct guards, each matching his man on the sheet, each standing, talking, turning and charging in his own manner; clean Japanese lip sync on the four spoken lines; the five faces turning hard together readable in one frame; the flying kick a single clear arc with the man flying straight back and Adam landing on both feet; the elbow a distinct event dropping a second man; the block, the three body shots and the chin four distinct readable events on one man; the slow-motion straight punch readable through the drift, the jaw deforming and the head snapping back; the duck, the groin shot, the fold, the knee and the backward fall five distinct readable events on the last man; Adam readable from the ground, towering, the coat snapping straight; the pistol appearing only at the end, swinging down into the low frame; consistent frame rate, no ghosting, no duplicated limbs or pistols.




POSITIVE CONSTRAINTS

One continuous handheld take, 30 seconds, one 50 mm lens, one speed ramp at 14.5–16.5s, the camera in the operator's bare hands and shaking hard in every frame of the take — jittering and lurching during the talk, smearing on the whips, slamming with his run, thrown by each impact, clipping the wall once, knocked once by a passing man, heaving in the slow motion, jerked upward with the knee, falling to the carpet at 20.5s and trembling there, crawling after Adam along the floor to the cut — with the horizon swinging ten to twenty degrees and never level. Six people in the scene: Adam and five unarmed guards, everyone's hands empty until the pistol at 26s. The five stand in the middle of the corridor facing each other in a loose ring for the first four and a half seconds and talk in Japanese — four short lines exactly as written, nothing else spoken in the scene; Adam appears at the far end, 20 m away, at 4.5s, walking; the camera whips to him and straight back, and at 5.5–6.5s all five faces turn hard together in one frame and the men break at 6.5s; Adam breaks into a run at the same moment. The camera runs behind the charging men at 6.5–9.5s, reaches the plain wall on Adam's left at 9.5s and stays on Adam's left three-quarter for the fight, drops to the carpet at 20.5s and shoots Adam from the ground, from below, for the coat and the breath, and crawls after him along the floor from 23.5s; every angle a three-quarter or a profile and none of them held. The fight, in this order, one man per event: at 8.5s Adam leaps and his flying boot drives into GUARD 2's chest and jaw — GUARD 2 flies two metres back and lands flat, and Adam lands on both feet; at 10s GUARD 4 arrives inside Adam's reach and Adam's right elbow crosses his jaw — GUARD 4 drops on his side; at 11.5s GUARD 3 throws a right — Adam's left forearm knocks it off its line and he lands three short strikes to the body — hook under the ribs, straight to the solar plexus, hook to the far ribs — and a fourth, a straight lead to the chin, and GUARD 3 drops on his back; at 14.5–16.5s, in slow motion, GUARD 5 runs at Adam and Adam's straight lead lands on his jaw while his shoulder is still loading — GUARD 5 falls rigid onto his back, unconscious; at 17s GUARD 1 swings a huge right — Adam drops under it on folded knees and drives his right fist straight up into GUARD 1's groin, GUARD 1 clamps both hands between his legs and folds double, and Adam's hands drag his head down onto a rising right knee — GUARD 1's head snaps back, his body straightens off the knee and he falls over backward flat on his back, out. All five stay where they fell, clothes whole, skin clean, carpet clean. Adam is completely still in his narrow side-on stance for a full second at 19.5–20.5s while the camera keeps trembling around him; from 20.5s the camera is on the carpet: he shakes his coat out hard by both lapels, squares it on his shoulders and exhales, all seen from the ground and below; then he walks down the corridor over the bodies with the camera crawling and shaking after him along the floor; at 26s, walking, his right hand draws the Desert Eagle from inside the left breast of his coat and it swings down into the low frame and hangs muzzle-down at his thigh to the cut. The pistol is seen only from 26s on and is never fired. Hard cut at 30 on his back and the pistol mid-stride, the frame still shaking on the carpet.




FIGHT STYLE LOCK: this fight has exactly one leap — the flying kick into the first man — and exactly one knee — the rising knee into the last man's face; every other strike travels a short straight line launched from where the hands already rest at guard — the first visible motion of a limb IS the strike itself; between exchanges the fighter is completely still in his narrow side-on stance; the fight is short straight lines and long stillness.
